<div class="subch">General</div>


<span id="2b01" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.01 Application of Regulatory Signs


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.01 &middot; manual page 67 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b01">CA amended: 2B.01</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.01/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Regulatory signs shall be used to inform road users of selected traffic laws or regulations and to indicate the applicability of the legal requirements.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Regulatory signs shall be installed at or near where the regulations apply. The signs shall clearly indicate the requirements imposed by the regulations and shall be designed and installed to provide adequate visibility and legibility in order to obtain compliance.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> Regulatory signs shall be retroreflective or illuminated (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2a/#2a21">Section 2A.21</a>).</p>
</div>

<span id="2b02" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.02 Design of Regulatory Signs


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.02 &middot; manual page 67 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b02">CA amended: 2B.02</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.02/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Regulatory signs shall be rectangular unless specifically designated otherwise in this Manual. Regulatory signs shall be designed in accordance with the sizes, shapes, colors, and legends contained in the “Standard Highway Signs” publication (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/1a/#1a05">Section 1A.05</a>).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The use of educational plaques to supplement symbol signs is described in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2a/#2a09">Section 2A.09</a>.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The use of LEDs in the border or legend of regulatory signs is described in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2a/#2a12">Section 2A.12</a>.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> LED signs displaying a part-time prohibitory message incorporating a red circle and diagonal of a static sign shall display a red symbol that approximates the same red circle and diagonal as closely as possible. The symbol of the action to be prohibited shall be displayed in white LEDs on a black background.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> A regulatory sign displayed entirely with LEDs and incorporated within the border of a larger full- matrix changeable message sign shall display the regulatory sign legend in the size, shape, color, and legend of the standard regulatory sign.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b03" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.03 Size of Regulatory Signs


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.03 &middot; manual page 67 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b03">CA amended: 2B.03</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.03/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Except as provided in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2a/#2a07">Section 2A.07</a>, the minimum sizes for regulatory signs shall be as shown in <a href="#fig-2b-1">Table 2B-1</a>.</p>
</div>
<div class="mfig-inline single"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-1"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0067.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0067.webp" alt="Table 2B-1. Support: 02 Section 2A.07 contains information regarding the applicability of the various columns in"></a><figcaption>Table 2B-1. Support: 02 Section 2A.07 contains information regarding the applicability of the various columns in <span class="src-page">p 67</span></figcaption></figure></div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2a/#2a07">Section 2A.07</a> contains information regarding the applicability of the various columns in <a href="#fig-2b-1">Table 2B-1</a>.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> Except as provided in Paragraphs 5 and 6 of this Section, the minimum sizes for regulatory signs facing traffic on multi-lane conventional roads shall be as shown in the Multi-Lane column of <a href="#fig-2b-1">Table 2B-1</a>.</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> The minimum size of regulatory signs applied on low-volume rural roads with operating speeds of 30 mph or less shall be as shown in the Minimum column of <a href="#fig-2b-1">Table 2B-1</a>.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> Where the posted speed limit is 35 mph or less on a multi-lane highway or street, other than for a STOP sign, the minimum size shown in the Single Lane column in <a href="#fig-2b-1">Table 2B-1</a> may be used.</p>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> Where a regulatory sign, other than a STOP sign, is placed on the left-hand side of a multi-lane roadway in addition to the installation of the same regulatory sign on the right-hand side or the roadway, the minimum size shown in the Single Lane column in <a href="#fig-2b-1">Table 2B-1</a> may be used for both the sign on the right-hand side and the sign on the left-hand side of the roadway.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> The minimum sizes for regulatory signs facing traffic on exit and entrance ramps at major interchanges connecting an Expressway or Freeway with an Expressway or Freeway (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2e/#2e11">Section 2E.11</a>) should be as shown in the column of <a href="#fig-2b-1">Table 2B-1</a> that corresponds to the mainline roadway classification (Expressway or Freeway). If a minimum size is not provided in the Freeway column, the minimum size in the Expressway column should be used. If a minimum size is not provided in the Freeway or Expressway Column, the size in the Oversized column should be used.</p>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> The minimum sizes for all regulatory signs facing traffic on exit and entrance ramps at all other classifications of interchanges (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2e/#2e11">Section 2E.11</a>) should be the sizes shown in <a href="#fig-2b-1">Table 2B-1</a> in the Conventional Road Single Lane column for single-lane ramps and in the Multi-Lane column for multi-lane ramps.</p>
</div>
<div class="mfig-grid"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-1"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0068.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0068.webp" alt="Table 2B-1. <span id="2b04" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.04 STOP Sign (R1-1) and ALL-WAY Plaque (R1-3P)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.04 &middot; manual page 74 &middot; <span class="badge muted">no CA counterpart found</span> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.04/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> When it is determined that a full stop is always required on an approach to an intersection, a STOP (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-1.svg">R1-1</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-1">Figure 2B-1</a>) shall be used.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Secondary legends shall not be used on STOP sign faces.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The STOP sign shall not be displayed using a changeable message sign.</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> At intersections where all approaches are controlled by STOP signs (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b12">Section 2B.12</a>), an ALL-WAY (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-3p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-3P.svg">R1-3P</a>) supplemental plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-1">Figure 2B-1</a>) shall be mounted below each STOP sign. The ALL-WAY plaque shall have a white legend and border on a red background.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> Supplemental plaques with legends such as 2-WAY, 3-WAY, 4-WAY, or other numbers of ways shall not be used with STOP signs.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> The use of the CROSS TRAFFIC DOES NOT STOP (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w4-4p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W4-4P.svg">W4-4P</a> series) and other plaques with variations of this legend is described in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2c/#2c66">Section 2C.66</a>.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> The TRAFFIC FROM LEFT (RIGHT) DOES NOT STOP (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w4-4ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W4-4aP.svg">W4-4aP</a>) plaque or ONCOMING TRAFFIC DOES NOT STOP (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w4-4bp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W4-4bP.svg">W4-4bP</a>) plaque should be used at intersections where STOP signs control all but one approach to the intersection, unless the only non-stopped approach is from a one-way street.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> The EXCEPT RIGHT TURN (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-10p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-10P.svg">R1-10P</a>) plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-1">Figure 2B-1</a>) may be mounted below the STOP sign if an engineering study determines that a special combination of geometry and traffic volumes is present that makes it possible for right-turning traffic on the approach to be allowed to enter the intersection without stopping.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> The design and application of Stop Beacons are described in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/4s/#4s05">Section 4S.05</a>.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b05" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.05 YIELD Sign (R1-2)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.05 &middot; manual page 74 &middot; <a class="badge ca-same" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b05">CA MUTCD 2026: 2B.05</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.05/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The YIELD sign requires road users to yield the right-of-way to other traffic on certain approaches to an intersection or on a two way approach to a one way section of roadway, such as a narrow bridge or underpass. Vehicles controlled by a YIELD sign need to slow down to a speed that is reasonable for the existing conditions or stop when necessary to avoid interfering with conflicting traffic.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The YIELD (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-2.svg">R1-2</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-1">Figure 2B-1</a>) shall not be displayed using a changeable message sign.</p>
</div>

<div class="subch">Signing For Right-Of-Way At Intersections</div>

<div class="mfig-grid"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-1"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0074.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0074.webp" alt="Figure 2B-1. STOP and YIELD Signs and Plaques"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-1. STOP and YIELD Signs and Plaques <span class="src-page">p 74</span></figcaption></figure></div>

<span id="2b06" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.06 General Considerations


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.06 &middot; manual page 75 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b06">CA amended: 2B.06</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.06/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Unsignalized intersections represent the most common form of intersection right-of-way control. Selection of control type might be impacted by specific requirements of State law or local ordinances.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Roundabouts and traffic circles are circular intersection designs and are not traffic control devices. The decision to convert an intersection from a conventional intersection to a circular intersection is an engineering design decision and not a traffic control device decision. As such, criteria for conversion from a conventional intersection to a circular intersection are not included in the MUTCD.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The type of traffic control used at an unsignalized intersection should be the least restrictive that provides appropriate levels of safety and efficiency for all road users.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> Some types of right-of-way control that can exist at an unsignalized intersection in order from the least restrictive to the most restrictive are the following: A. No intersection control (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b09">Section 2B.09</a>): There are no right-of-way traffic control devices on any of the approaches to the intersection. B. Yield control (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b10">Section 2B.10</a>): YIELD signs are placed on all approaches (for a circular intersection), on opposing approaches for a four-leg intersection, on a single approach for a three-leg intersection, or in the median of a divided highway. The YIELD signs are placed on the minor road. C. Minor road stop control (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b11">Section 2B.11</a>): STOP signs are typically placed on opposing approaches (for a four-leg intersection) or on a single approach (for a three-leg intersection). The STOP signs are normally placed on the minor road. <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b07">Section 2B.07</a> contains guidance on selecting the minor road. D. All-way stop control (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b12">Section 2B.12</a>): STOP signs are placed on all approaches to the intersection.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> When selecting a form of intersection control, the following factors should be considered: A. Motor vehicle, bicycle, and pedestrian traffic volumes on all approaches; where the term units/day or units/hour is indicated, it should be the total of motor vehicle, bicycle, and pedestrian volume; B. Driver yielding behavior with regard to all modes of conflicting traffic, including bicyclists and pedestrians; C. Number and angle of approaches; D. Approach speeds; E. Sight distance available on each approach; F. Reported crash experience; and G. The presence of a grade crossing near the intersection.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> YIELD or STOP signs shall not be used for speed control.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> Appropriate traffic calming or other speed control measures are available to control vehicle speeds, such as those that do not have the potential to diminish the effectiveness of traffic control devices when used for their specified purpose.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> Because the potential for conflicting commands could create driver confusion, YIELD or STOP signs shall not be used in conjunction with any traffic control signal operation, except in the following cases: A. If the signal indication for an approach is a flashing red at all times; B. If a minor street or driveway is located within or adjacent to the area controlled by the traffic control signal, but does not require separate traffic signal control because an extremely low potential for conflict exists; or C. If a channelized turn lane is separated from the adjacent travel lanes by an island and the channelized turn lane is not controlled by a traffic control signal.</p>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> STOP signs and YIELD signs shall not be installed on different approaches to the same unsignalized intersection if those approaches conflict with or oppose each other, except as provided for in Items A and B in Paragraph 3 of <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b10">Section 2B.10</a>.</p>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> Portable or part-time STOP or YIELD signs shall not be used except for emergency and temporary traffic control zone purposes.</p>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> A portable or part-time (folding) STOP sign that is manually placed into view and manually removed from view shall not be used during a power outage to control a signalized approach unless the maintaining agency establishes that the signal indication that will first be displayed to that approach upon restoration of power is a flashing red signal indication and that the portable STOP sign will be manually removed from view prior to resuming stop-and-go operation of the traffic control signal.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> A portable or part-time (folding) STOP sign that is electrically or mechanically operated such that it only displays the stop message during a power outage and ceases to display the stop message upon restoration of power may be used during a power outage to control a signalized approach.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">13</span> The use of STOP signs at grade crossings is described in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/8b/#8b04">Sections 8B.04</a> and 8B.05.</p>
<p><span class="pn">14</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/9b/#9b01">Section 9B.01</a> contains provisions regarding the assignment of priority where a shared-use path crosses a roadway.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b07" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.07 Determining the Minor Road for Unsignalized Intersections


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.07 &middot; manual page 76 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b07">CA amended: 2B.07</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.07/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The selection of the minor road to be controlled by YIELD or STOP signs should be based on one or more of the following criteria: A. A roadway intersecting a designated through or numbered highway, B. A roadway with the lower functional classification, C. A roadway with the lower traffic volume, D. A roadway with the lower speed limit, and/or E. A roadway that intersects with a roadway that has a higher priority for one or more modes of travel.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> When two roadways that have relatively equal volumes, speeds, and/or other characteristics intersect,the following factors should be considered in selecting the minor road for installation of YIELD or STOP signs: A. Controlling the direction that conflicts the most with established pedestrian crossing activity or school walking routes; B. Controlling the direction that has obscured vision, dips, or bumps that already require drivers to use lower operating speeds; and C. Controlling the direction that has the best sight distance from a controlled position to observe conflicting traffic.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b08" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.08 Right-of-Way Intersection Control Considerations


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.08 &middot; manual page 76 &middot; <span class="badge rev">Revision 1 page</span> &middot; <a class="badge ca-same" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b08">CA MUTCD 2026: 2B.08</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.08/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Before converting to a more restrictive form of right-of-way control at an unsignalized intersection, the following alternative treatments to address safety, operational, or other concerns should be among those to be considered: A. Where yield or stop controlled, installing Yield Ahead or Stop Ahead signs on the appropriate approaches to the intersection; B. Removing parking on one or more approaches; C. Removing sight distance obstructions; D. Installing signs along the major street to warn road users approaching the intersection; E. Relocating the stop line(s) and making other changes to improve the sight distance at the intersection; F. Installing measures designed to reduce speeds on the approaches; G. Installing an Intersection Control Beacon (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/4s/#4s02">Section 4S.02</a>) or Stop Beacon (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/4s/#4s05">Section 4S.05</a>) at the intersection to supplement STOP sign control; H. Installing a Warning Beacon (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/4s/#4s03">Section 4S.03</a>) on warning signs in advance of a stop-controlled intersection on major-street and/or minor-street approaches; I. Adding one or more lanes on a minor-street approach to reduce the number of vehicles per lane on the approach; J. Revising the geometrics at the intersection to channelize vehicular movements and reduce the time required for a vehicle to complete a movement, which could also assist pedestrians; K. Revising the geometrics at the intersection to add pedestrian median refuge islands and/or curb extensions; L. Installing roadway lighting if a disproportionate number of crashes occur at night; M. Restricting one or more turning movements on a full-time or part-time basis if alternate routes are available; N. Installing on the major street a pedestrian-actuated device: Warning Beacon (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/4s/#4s03">Section 4S.03</a>), rectangular rapid-flashing beacon (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/4l/#4l01">Section 4L.01</a>), or In-Roadway Warning Lights (see Chapter 4U), if pedestrian safety is the major concern; O. If the warrant is satisfied, installing all-way stop control; P. Installing a pedestrian hybrid beacon (see Chapter 4J) on the major street to address pedestrian safety; Q. Installing a circular intersection; and R. Employing other alternatives, depending on conditions at the intersection.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b09" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.09 No Intersection Control


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.09 &middot; manual page 77 &middot; <span class="badge rev">Revision 1 page</span> &middot; <a class="badge ca-same" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b09">CA MUTCD 2026: 2B.09</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.09/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The decision not to use intersection control should be based on engineering judgment.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The following factors may be considered: A. Intersection sight distance is adequate on all approaches. B. All approaches to the intersection are a single lane and there are no separate turn lanes. C. The combined motor vehicle, bicycle, and pedestrian volume (existing or projected) entering the intersection from all approaches averages less than 1,000 units per day or 80 units in the peak hour. D. There are no marked crosswalks or bicycle lanes on any approach. E. None of the approaches to the intersection are for a through highway, main road, or higher functional classification. F. The angle of intersection is between 90 and 75 degrees. G. The functional classification of the intersecting streets is either the intersection of two local streets or the intersection of a local street with a collector street.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b10" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.10 Yield Control


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.10 &middot; manual page 77 &middot; <span class="badge rev">Revision 1 page</span> &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b10">CA amended: 2B.10</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.10/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> At intersections where a full stop is not necessary at all times, consideration should first be given to using less restrictive measures such as YIELD signs.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Yield control should be considered when engineering judgment indicates that all of the following conditions exist: A. Intersection sight distance is adequate on the approaches to be controlled by YIELD signs. B. All approaches to the intersection are a single lane and there are no separate turn lanes. C. One of the following crash-related criteria applies: 1. For changing from no intersection control to yield control, there have been two or more reported crashes in the previous 12 months that are susceptible to correction by the installation of a YIELD sign. 2. For changing from minor road stop control to yield control, there have been two or fewer reported crashes in the previous 12 months. D. The combined motor vehicle, bicycle, and pedestrian volume entering the intersection averages less than 1,800 units per day or 140 units in the peak hour. E. The angle of intersection is between 90 and 75 degrees. F. The functional classification of the intersecting streets is either the intersection of two local streets or the intersection of a local street with a collector street.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> YIELD signs may be installed at an intersection when any of the following conditions apply: A. At the second intersection of a divided highway crossing or median break functioning as two separate intersections (see <a href="#fig-2b-19">Figure 2B-19</a>). In this case, a YIELD sign may be installed at the entrance to the second intersection. B. For a channelized turn lane that is separated from the adjacent travel lanes by an island, even if the adjacent lanes at the intersection are controlled by a highway traffic control signal or by a STOP sign. C. At an intersection where a special problem exists and where engineering judgment indicates the problem to be susceptible to correction by the use of the YIELD sign. D. Facing the entering roadway for a merge-type movement if engineering judgment indicates that control is needed because acceleration geometry and/or sight distance is not adequate for merging traffic operation. E. On low-volume rural roads if engineering judgment indicates that a YIELD sign would provide adequate control.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> The YIELD signs should be installed on opposing minor-street approaches (for a four-leg intersection) or on the minor-street approach (for a three-leg intersection). When two intersecting roadways have relatively equal volumes, speeds, and other characteristics, yield control should be installed on the approach that conflicts the most with established pedestrian crossing activity, school walking routes, or bicycle crossing activity.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> A YIELD sign shall be used to require road users to yield the right-of-way to other traffic at the entrance to a roundabout. YIELD signs at roundabouts shall be used to control the approach roadways and shall not be used to control the circulatory roadway.</p>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> YIELD signs shall not be placed on all of the approaches to an intersection, except at roundabouts.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b11" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.11 Minor Road Stop Control


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.11 &middot; manual page 78 &middot; <a class="badge ca-same" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b11">CA MUTCD 2026: 2B.11</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.11/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Stop control on the minor-road approach or approaches to an intersection should be considered when engineering judgment indicates that one or more of the following conditions exist: A. A restricted view exists that requires road users to stop in order to adequately observe conflicting traffic on the through street or highway. B. Crash records indicate that: 1. For a four-leg intersection, there are three or more reported crashes in a 12-month period or six or more reported crashes in a 36-month period. The crashes should be susceptible to correction by installation of minor-road stop control. 2. For a three-leg intersection, there are three or more reported crashes in a 12-month period or five or more reported crashes in a 36-month period. The crashes should be susceptible to correction by installation of minor-road stop control. C. The intersection is of a lower functional classification road with a higher functional classification road. D. Conditions that previously supported the installation of all-way stop control no longer exist.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> On low-volume rural roads, a STOP sign should be considered at an intersection where engineering judgment indicates that Item C in Paragraph 1 of this Section is applicable or where the intersection has inadequate sight distance for the operating vehicle speeds.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b12" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.12 All-Way Stop Control


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.12 &middot; manual page 78 &middot; <a class="badge ca-same" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b12">CA MUTCD 2026: 2B.12</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.12/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The provisions in the following sections describe warrants for the recommended engineering study to determine all-way stop control. Warrants are not a substitute for engineering judgment. The fact that a warrant for a particular traffic control device is met is not conclusive justification to install or not install all-way stop control. Because each intersection will have unique characteristics that affect its operational performance or safety, it is the engineering study for a given intersection that is ultimately the basis for a decision to install or not install all-way stop control.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> All-way stop controls at intersections with substantially differing approach volumes can reduce the effectiveness of these devices for all roadway users.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The decision to establish all-way stop control at an unsignalized intersection should be based on an engineering study. The engineering study for all-way stop control should include an analysis of factors related to the existing operation and safety at the intersection, the potential to improve these conditions, and the applicable factors contained in the following all-way stop control warrants: A. All-Way Stop Control Warrant A: Crash Experience (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b13">Section 2B.13</a>) B. All-Way Stop Control Warrant B: Sight Distance (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b14">Section 2B.14</a>) C. All-Way Stop Control Warrant C: Transition to Signal Control or Transition to Yield Control at a Circular Intersection (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b15">Section 2B.15</a>) D. All-Way Stop Control Warrant D: 8-Hour Volume (Vehicles, Pedestrians, Bicycles) (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b16">Section 2B.16</a>) E. All-Way Stop Control Warrant E: Other Factors (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b17">Section 2B.17</a>)</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> The decision to install all-way stop control on site roadways open to public travel may be based on engineering judgment.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> The satisfaction of an all-way stop control warrant or warrants shall not in itself require the installation of all-way stop control at an unsignalized intersection.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b13" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.13 All-Way Stop Control Warrant A: Crash Experience


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.13 &middot; manual page 79 &middot; <a class="badge ca-same" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b13">CA MUTCD 2026: 2B.13</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.13/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> All-way stop control may be installed at an intersection where an engineering study indicates that: A. For a four-leg intersection, there are five or more reported crashes in a 12-month period or six or more reported crashes in a 36-month period that were of a type susceptible to correction by the installation of all-way stop control. B. For a three-leg intersection, there are four or more reported crashes in a 12-month period or five or more reported crashes in a 36-month period that were of a type susceptible to correction by the installation of all-way stop control.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b14" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.14 All-Way Stop Control Warrant B: Sight Distance


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.14 &middot; manual page 79 &middot; <a class="badge ca-same" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b14">CA MUTCD 2026: 2B.14</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.14/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> All-way stop control may be installed at an intersection where an engineering study indicates that sight distance on the minor-road approaches controlled by a STOP sign is not adequate for a vehicle to turn onto or cross the major (uncontrolled) road.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> At such a location, a road user, after stopping, cannot see conflicting traffic and is not able to negotiate the intersection unless conflicting cross traffic is also required to stop.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b15" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.15 All-Way Stop Control Warrant C: Transition to Signal Control or Transition to Yield Control at a Circular Intersection


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.15 &middot; manual page 79 &middot; <a class="badge ca-same" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b15">CA MUTCD 2026: 2B.15</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.15/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> All-way stop control may be installed at locations where all-way stop control is an interim measure that can be installed to control traffic while arrangements are being made for the installation of a traffic control signal (see Chapter 4C) at the intersection or for the installation of yield control at a circular intersection.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b16" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.16 All-Way Stop Control Warrant D: 8-Hour Volume (Vehicles, Pedestrians, Bicycles)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.16 &middot; manual page 79 &middot; <a class="badge ca-same" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b16">CA MUTCD 2026: 2B.16</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.16/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> All-way stop control may be installed at an intersection where an engineering study indicates: A. The combined motor vehicle, bicycle, and pedestrian volume entering the intersection from the major- street approaches is at least 300 units per hour for each of any 8 hours of a typical day; and B. The combined motor vehicle, bicycle, and pedestrian volume entering the intersection from the minor- street approaches is at least 200 units per hour for each of any of the same 8 hours.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> If the 85th-percentile approach speed of the major-street traffic exceeds 40 mph, the minimum vehicular volume warrants may be reduced to 70 percent of the values given in Items A and B in Paragraph 1 of this Section.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b17" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.17 All-Way Stop Control Warrant E: Other Factors


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.17 &middot; manual page 79 &middot; <a class="badge ca-same" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b17">CA MUTCD 2026: 2B.17</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.17/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> All-way stop control may be installed at an intersection where an engineering study indicates that all-way stop control is needed due to other factors not addressed in the other all-way stop control warrants. Such other factors may include, but are not limited to, the following: A. The need to control left-turn conflicts, B. An intersection of two residential neighborhood collector (through) streets of similar design and operating characteristics where all-way stop control would improve traffic operational characteristics of the intersection, or C. Where pedestrian and/or bicyclist movements support the installation of all-way stop control.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b18" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.18 STOP Sign or YIELD Sign Placement


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.18 &middot; manual page 79 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b18">CA amended: 2B.18</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.18/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The STOP or YIELD sign shall be installed on the near side of the intersection on the right-hand side of the approach to which it applies. When the STOP or YIELD sign is installed at this required location and the sign visibility is restricted, a Stop Ahead sign (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2c/#2c35">Section 2C.35</a>) shall be installed in advance of the STOP sign or a Yield Ahead sign (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2c/#2c35">Section 2C.35</a>) shall be installed in advance of the YIELD sign.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The STOP or YIELD sign shall be located as close as practicable to the intersection it regulates, while optimizing its visibility to the road user it is intended to regulate.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> STOP signs and YIELD signs shall not be mounted on the same post.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2a/#2a05">Section 2A.05</a> contains information about mounting signs back-to-back with a STOP or YIELD sign.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> STOP or YIELD signs should not be placed farther than 50 feet from the edge of the pavement of the intersected roadway (see Drawing F in <a href="#fig-2a-3">Figure 2A-3</a>).</p>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> Supplemental plaques used in conjunction with a STOP or YIELD sign should be limited to those specified for such use in this Manual.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> Where drivers proceeding straight ahead must yield to traffic approaching from the opposite direction, such as at a one-lane bridge, a TO ONCOMING TRAFFIC (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-2ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-2aP.svg">R1-2aP</a>) plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-1">Figure 2B-1</a>) may be mounted below the YIELD sign.</p>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> Where drivers must yield to traffic in a multi-lane roundabout, a TO TRAFFIC IN CIRCLE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-2bp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-2bP.svg">R1-2bP</a>) or TO ALL LANES (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-2cp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-2cP.svg">R1-2cP</a>) plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-1">Figure 2B-1</a>) may be mounted below the YIELD sign.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> <a href="#fig-2a-3">Figure 2A-3</a> shows examples of some typical placements of STOP signs and YIELD signs.</p>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2a/#2a13">Section 2A.13</a> contains additional information about separate and combined mounting of other signs with STOP or YIELD signs.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> Stop lines that are used to supplement a STOP sign should be located as described in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/3b/#3b19">Section 3B.19</a>. Yield lines that are used to supplement a YIELD sign should be located as described in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/3b/#3b19">Section 3B.19</a>.</p>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> Where there is a marked crosswalk at the intersection, the STOP sign should be installed in advance of the edge of the crosswalk that is nearest to the approaching traffic.</p>
<p><span class="pn">13</span> Except at roundabouts and channelized right-turn lanes, where there is a marked crosswalk at the intersection, the YIELD sign should be installed in advance of the edge of the crosswalk that is nearest to the approaching traffic.</p>
<p><span class="pn">14</span> Where two roads intersect at an acute angle, the STOP or YIELD sign should be positioned at an angle, or shielded, so that the legend is out of view of traffic to which it does not apply.</p>
<p><span class="pn">15</span> If a raised splitter island is available on the left-hand side of a multi-lane roundabout approach, an additional YIELD sign should be placed on the left-hand side of the approach.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">16</span> If a raised splitter island is available on the left-hand side of a single-lane roundabout approach, an additional YIELD sign may be placed on the left-hand side of the approach.</p>
<p><span class="pn">17</span> At wide-throat intersections or where two or more approach lanes of traffic exist on the signed approach, an additional STOP or YIELD sign may be installed on the left-hand side of the road and/or a stop or yield line may be used to improve observance of the right-of-way control. At channelized intersections or at divided roadways separated by a median or divisional island, the additional STOP or YIELD sign may be placed on a channelizing island, or in the median or on the divisional island. An additional STOP or YIELD sign may also be placed overhead facing the approach at the intersection to improve observance of the right-of-way control.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">18</span> More than one STOP sign or more than one YIELD sign shall not be placed on the same support facing in the same direction.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">19</span> For a yield-controlled channelized right-turn movement onto a roadway without an acceleration lane and for an entrance ramp onto a freeway or expressway without an acceleration lane, a NO MERGE AREA (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w4-5ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W4-5aP.svg">W4-5aP</a>) supplemental plaque (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2c/#2c45">Section 2C.45</a>) may be mounted below a Yield Ahead (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w3-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W3-2.svg">W3-2</a>) sign and/or below a YIELD (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-2.svg">R1-2</a>) sign when engineering judgment indicates that road users would expect an acceleration lane to be present.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b19" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.19 Yield Here To Pedestrians Signs and Stop Here For Pedestrians Signs (R1-5 Series)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.19 &middot; manual page 81 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b19">CA amended: 2B.19</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.19/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5.svg">R1-5</a> series signs are intended to mitigate the scenario that can place pedestrians at risk by blocking other drivers’ view of pedestrians and by blocking the pedestrians’ view of the vehicles approaching in the adjacent lanes.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5.svg">R1-5</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5a.svg">R1-5a</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5b/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5b.svg">R1-5b</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5c/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5c.svg">R1-5c</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5d/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5d.svg">R1-5d</a>, and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5e/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5e.svg">R1-5e</a>) signs (see <a href="#fig-2b-2">Figure 2B-2</a>) shall be used if yield (stop) lines are used in advance of a marked crosswalk only where it crosses an uncontrolled multi-lane approach. The Stop Here for Pedestrians signs shall only be used where the law specifically requires that a driver must stop for a pedestrian in a crosswalk. The legend STATE LAW shall not be displayed on the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5.svg">R1-5</a> series signs.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> If yield (stop) lines and Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians signs are used in advance of a crosswalk that crosses an uncontrolled multi-lane approach, the signs should be placed 20 to 50 feet in advance of the nearest edge of the crosswalk (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/3b/#3b19">Section 3B.19</a> and <a href="#fig-3b-16">Figure 3B-16</a>).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> When used with a School Crossing assembly within school zones (see Part 7), the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5a.svg">R1-5a</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5c/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5c.svg">R1-5c</a> signs shall be used in place of the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5.svg">R1-5</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5b/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5b.svg">R1-5b</a> signs in accordance with Paragraph 2 of this Section.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> When used with a Trail Crossing assembly (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2c/#2c54">Section 2C.54</a>), the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5d/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5d.svg">R1-5d</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5e/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5e.svg">R1-5e</a> signs shall be used in place of the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5.svg">R1-5</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5b/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5b.svg">R1-5b</a> signs in accordance with Paragraph 2 of this Section.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> When Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians signs are provided in advance of a crosswalk across an multi-lane approach, parking should be prohibited in the area between the yield (stop) line and the crosswalk.</p>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> Yield (stop) lines and Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians signs should not be used in advance of crosswalks that cross an approach to or departure from a roundabout.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians signs may be used in accordance with Paragraphs 2 through 4 of this Section even if yield (stop) lines are not used.</p>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> A Pedestrian Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w11-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W11-2.svg">W11-2</a>) warning sign may be placed overhead or may be post-mounted with a diagonal downward-pointing arrow (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w16-7p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W16-7P.svg">W16-7P</a>) plaque at the crosswalk location where Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians signs have been installed in advance of the crosswalk.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> If a <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w11-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W11-2.svg">W11-2</a> sign is post-mounted at the crosswalk location where a Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians sign is used on the approach, the Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians sign shall not be placed on the same post as the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w11-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W11-2.svg">W11-2</a> sign.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> An advance Pedestrian Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w11-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W11-2.svg">W11-2</a>) warning sign with an AHEAD or a distance supplemental plaque may be used in conjunction with a Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians sign on the approach to the same crosswalk.</p>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> In-Street Pedestrian Crossing signs and Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians signs may be used together at the same crosswalk.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b20" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.20 In-Street and Overhead Pedestrian and Trail Crossing Signs (R1-6 and R1-9 Series)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.20 &middot; manual page 81 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b20">CA amended: 2B.20</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.20/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The In-Street Pedestrian Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-6/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-6.svg">R1-6</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-6a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-6a.svg">R1-6a</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-2">Figure 2B-2</a>), In-Street Trail Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-6d/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-6d.svg">R1-6d</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-6e/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-6e.svg">R1-6e</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-2">Figure 2B-2</a>), the Overhead Pedestrian Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-9/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-9.svg">R1-9</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-9a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-9a.svg">R1-9a</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-2">Figure 2B-2</a>), or the Overhead Trail Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-9d/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-9d.svg">R1-9d</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-9e/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-9e.svg">R1-9e</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-2">Figure 2B-2</a>) may be used to remind road users of laws regarding right-of-way at an unsignalized crosswalk. The legend STATE LAW may be displayed at the top of the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-6/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-6.svg">R1-6</a> series and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-9/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-9.svg">R1-9</a> series signs if applicable. On the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-6/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-6.svg">R1-6</a> series signs, the legends STOP or YIELD may be used instead of the appropriate STOP sign or YIELD sign symbol.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Highway agencies may develop and apply criteria for determining the applicability of In-Street Pedestrian Crossing signs.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The STOP FOR legend shall only be used in States where the State law specifically requires that a driver must stop for a pedestrian or a bicyclist in a crosswalk.</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> If used, In-Street Pedestrian or Trail Crossing signs shall only be placed in the roadway at the crosswalk location on the center line, on a median island, on a lane line, or on an edge line.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> The In-Street Pedestrian or Trail Crossing sign shall not be post-mounted on the left-hand or right- hand side of the roadway.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/3i/#3i02">Section 3I.02</a> contains information about the use of tubular markers to provide additional emphasis for a pedestrian crossing.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> If used, the Overhead Pedestrian or Trail Crossing sign shall be placed over the roadway at the crosswalk location.</p>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> When used at an uncontrolled crossing, the In-Street or Overhead Pedestrian Crossing sign shall be used only as a supplement to a Pedestrian Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w11-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W11-2.svg">W11-2</a>) warning sign with a diagonal downward- pointing arrow (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w16-7p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W16-7P.svg">W16-7P</a>) plaque at the crosswalk location.</p>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> When used at an uncontrolled crossing, the In-Street or Overhead Trail Crossing sign shall be used only as a supplement to a Trail Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w11-15/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W11-15.svg">W11-15</a>) warning sign with a diagonal downward-pointing arrow (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w16-7p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W16-7P.svg">W16-7P</a>) plaque at the crosswalk location.</p>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> An In-Street or Overhead Pedestrian or Trail Crossing sign shall not be placed in advance of the crosswalk to educate road users about the State law prior to reaching the crosswalk, nor shall it be installed as an educational display that is not near any crosswalk.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> If an island (see Chapter 3J) is available, the In-Street Pedestrian or Trail Crossing sign, if used, should be placed on the island.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> In-Street Pedestrian or Trail Crossing signs may be mounted back-to-back in the median or on the center line of an undivided roadway.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">13</span> The In-Street Pedestrian or Trail Crossing sign and the Overhead Pedestrian Crossing or Trail sign shall not be used at crosswalks on approaches controlled by a traffic control signal, pedestrian hybrid beacon, or an emergency-vehicle hybrid beacon.</p>
<p><span class="pn">14</span> Except where the In-Street Crossing sign is placed on a physical island, the sign support shall be designed to bend over and then bounce back to its normal vertical position when struck by a vehicle.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">15</span> The In-Street and Overhead Pedestrian and Trail Crossing sign may be used at intersections or midblock pedestrian crossings with flashing beacons.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">16</span> The provisions of <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2a/#2a15">Section 2A.15</a> concerning mounting height are not applicable for the In-Street Pedestrian Crossing sign. <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2a/#2a18">Section 2A.18</a> contains information about sign mounting methods.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">17</span> The top of an In-Street Pedestrian or Trail Crossing sign shall be a maximum of 4 feet above the pavement surface. The top of an In-Street Pedestrian or Trail Crossing sign placed in an island shall be a maximum of 4 feet above the island surface.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">18</span> The In-Street Pedestrian Crossing or Trail Crossing signs may be used seasonally to prevent damage in winter because of plowing operations, and may be removed at night if the pedestrian activity at night is minimal.</p>
<p><span class="pn">19</span> Both sign mounting types, In-Street Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-6/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-6.svg">R1-6</a> series) signs and Overhead Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-9/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-9.svg">R1-9</a> series) signs, may be used together at the same crosswalk.</p>
</div>

<div class="subch">Speed Limit Signs And Plaques</div>


<span id="2b21" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.21 Speed Limit Sign (R2-1)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.21 &middot; manual page 84 &middot; <span class="badge rev">Revision 1 page</span> &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b21">CA amended: 2B.21</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.21/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> In general, the maximum speed limits applicable to rural and urban roads are established: A. Statutorily – a maximum speed limit applicable to a particular class of road, such as freeways or city streets, that is established by State law; or B. As speed zones – based on engineering studies.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> State statutory limits might restrict the maximum speed limit that can be established on a particular road, notwithstanding what an engineering study might indicate.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> Agencies with designated authorities to set speed limits, which include States, and sometimes local jurisdictions, can establish non-statutory speed limits or designate reduced speed zones using an engineering study. Setting appropriate speed limits is especially important to ensure safety for all road users in varying types of contexts, particularly on roadways where adjacent land use suggests that trips could be served by varied modes. These situations include urban and suburban non-freeway arterials or rural arterials that serve as main streets in smaller communities, consistent with the context classifications of urban core, urban, suburban, and rural towns found in “A Policy on Geometric Design of Highways and Streets,” 2018 Edition, AASHTO. When setting a speed limit, a range of factors such as land-use context, pedestrian and bicyclist activity, crash history, intersection spacing, driveway density, roadway geometry, roadside conditions, roadway functional classification, traffic volume, and observed speeds can influence the speed limit determined in the engineering study. The engineering study will determine which of the recommended factors will prevail in setting the speed limit.</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> Jurisdictions can use speed limit setting tools and methods such as expert systems and those consistent with the safe system approach as part of the required engineering study for a non-statutory speed limit. As speed limit setting tools vary, jurisdictions need to be aware of their limitations and advantages, possible variation between the tools and the need to explore gaps or weaknesses of tools, and weigh the output accordingly in consideration of setting speed limits.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> To achieve desired operating speeds, agencies often implement other speed management strategies concurrently with setting speed limits, such as traffic calming measures, geometric design features, speed safety cameras, and increased enforcement.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> Speed zones (other than statutory speed limits) shall only be established on the basis of an engineering study that has been performed in accordance with traffic engineering practices. The engineering study shall consider the roadway context.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> Among the factors that should be considered when conducting an engineering study for establishing or reevaluating speed limits within speed zones are the following: A. Roadway environment (such as roadside development, number and frequency of driveways and access points, and land use), functional classification, public transit volume and location or frequency of stops, parking practices, and pedestrian and bicycle facilities and activity; B. Roadway characteristics (such as lane widths, shoulder condition, grade, alignment, median type, and sight distance); C. Geographic context (such as an urban district, rural town center, non-urbanized rural area, or suburban area), and multi-modal trip generation; D. Reported crash experience for at least a 12-month period; E. Speed distribution of free-flowing vehicles including the pace, median (50th-percentile), and 85th-percentile speeds; and F. A review of past speed studies to identify any trends in operating speeds.</p>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> When the 85th-percentile speed is appreciably greater than the posted speed limit, and the roadway context does not support setting a higher speed limit, the engineering study should consider whether changes to geometric features, enforcement, and/or other speed-reduction countermeasures might improve compliance with the posted speed limit. A similar approach should be used if the results of past speed studies indicate that the 85th-percentile speed has consistently increased.</p>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> On urban and suburban arterials, and on rural arterials that serve as main streets through developed areas of communities, the 85th-percentile speed should not be used to set speed limits without consideration of all factors described in Paragraph 7 of this Section.</p>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> On a freeway, expressway, or rural highway (outside urbanized locations or conditions), the speed limit that is posted within a speed zone should be within 5 mph of the 85th-percentile speed of free-flowing motor-vehicle traffic under the following conditions: A. All factors described in Paragraph 7 of this Section have been considered and determined to be non- mitigating, and B. The measures described in Paragraph 8 of this Section have been considered to the extent practicable.</p>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> State and local agencies should conduct engineering studies to reevaluate non-statutory speed limits on segments of their roadways that have undergone significant changes since the last review (such as changes to roadway context, the addition or elimination of parking or driveways, changes in the number of travel lanes, changes in the configuration of bicycle lanes, changes to road geometrics, changes in traffic control signal coordination, or significant changes in traffic volumes).</p>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> Speed studies for signalized intersection approaches should be taken outside the influence area of the traffic control signal, which is generally considered to be approximately 1/2 mile, to avoid obtaining skewed results for the speed distribution. If the signal spacing is less than 1 mile, the speed study should be at approximately the middle of the segment.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">13</span> The Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-3">Figure 2B-3</a>) shall display the limit established by law, ordinance, regulation, or as adopted by the authorized agency based on an engineering study. The speed limits displayed shall be in multiples of 5 mph.</p>
<p><span class="pn">14</span> Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) signs, indicating speed limits for which posting is required by law, shall be located at the points of change from one speed limit to another.</p>
<p><span class="pn">15</span> At the downstream end of the section to which a particular speed limit applies, a Speed Limit sign showing the next speed limit shall be installed.</p>
<p><span class="pn">16</span> Speed Limit signs indicating the statutory speed limits shall be installed at entrances to the State and, where appropriate, at jurisdictional boundaries in urban areas.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">17</span> Additional Speed Limit signs should be installed beyond interchanges and major intersections and at other locations where it is necessary to remind road users of the speed limit that is applicable.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">18</span> The 2nd Edition of the “Traffic Control Devices Handbook,” 2013, Institute of Transportation Engineers” contains suggested criteria on the spacing of speed limit signs.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">19</span> If a jurisdiction has a policy of installing Speed Limit signs in accordance with statutory requirements only on the streets that enter a city, neighborhood, or residential area to indicate the speed limit that is applicable to the entire city, neighborhood, or residential area unless otherwise posted, a CITYWIDE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-5ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-5aP.svg">R2-5aP</a>), NEIGHBORHOOD (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-5bp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-5bP.svg">R2-5bP</a>), or RESIDENTIAL (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-5cp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-5cP.svg">R2-5cP</a>) plaque may be mounted above the Speed Limit sign and an UNLESS OTHERWISE POSTED (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-5p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-5P.svg">R2-5P</a>) plaque may be mounted below the Speed Limit sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-3">Figure 2B-3</a>).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">20</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2c/#2c40">Section 2C.40</a> contains information about the use of speed zone signs to inform road users of a reduced or variable speed zone to provide advance notice to comply with the posted speed limit ahead.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">21</span> If a <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w3-5b/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W3-5b.svg">W3-5b</a> sign is posted to provide notice of a variable speed zone, an END VARIABLE SPEED LIMIT (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-13/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-13.svg">R2-13</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-3">Figure 2B-3</a>) may be installed at the downstream end of the zone to provide notice to road users of the termination of the speed zone.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">22</span> If a <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w3-5c/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W3-5c.svg">W3-5c</a> sign is posted to provide notice of a truck speed zone, an END TRUCK SPEED LIMIT (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-14/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-14.svg">R2-14</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-3">Figure 2B-3</a>) shall be installed at the downstream end of the zone to provide notice to road users of the termination of the speed zone.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">23</span> An advisory speed plaque (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2c/#2c59">Section 2C.59</a>) mounted below a warning sign should be used to warn road users of an advisory speed for a roadway condition. A Speed Limit sign should not be used for this purpose.</p>
<p><span class="pn">24</span> Advance traffic control warning signs (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2c/#2c35">Section 2C.35</a>), intersection warning signs (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2c/#2c41">Section 2C.41</a>), and/or other traffic control devices are appropriate warning prior to a signalized intersection. A Speed Limit sign should not be used for this purpose.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">25</span> Two types of Speed Limit signs may be used: one to designate passenger car speeds, including any nighttime information or maximum or minimum speed limit that might apply; and the other to show any special speed limits for trucks and other vehicles.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">26</span> No more than three speed limits should be displayed on any one Speed Limit sign or assembly.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">27</span> A variable speed limit sign that changes the speed limit for traffic and ambient conditions may be installed provided that the appropriate speed limit is displayed at the proper times and locations in accordance with Paragraphs 9 and 10 of this Section.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">28</span> The variable speed limit sign legend “SPEED LIMIT” shall be a black legend on a white retroreflective background. The variable speed limit legend shall be displayed in white LEDs on an opaque black background.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">29</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2c/#2c13">Section 2C.13</a> contains information about the use of a Vehicle Speed Feedback plaque mounted below a Speed Limit sign that displays to approaching drivers the speed at which they are traveling.</p>
<p><span class="pn">30</span> Advisory speed signs and plaques are discussed in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2c/#2c12">Sections 2C.12</a> and 2C.59. Temporary traffic control zone speed signs are discussed in Part 6. The WORK ZONE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/g20-5ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/G20-5aP.svg">G20-5aP</a>) plaque intended for installation above a Speed Limit sign is discussed in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/6g/#6g08">Section 6G.08</a>. School Speed Limit signs are discussed in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/7b/#7b05">Section 7B.05</a>.</p>
</div>
<div class="mfig-grid"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-3"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0086.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0086.webp" alt="Figure 2B-3. Speed Limit Signs and Plaques Rev. 1"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-3. Speed Limit Signs and Plaques Rev. 1 <span class="src-page">p 86</span></figcaption></figure></div>

<span id="2b22" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.22 Vehicle Speed Limit Plaques (R2-2P Series)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.22 &middot; manual page 87 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b22">CA amended: 2B.22</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.22/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Where a special speed limit applies to certain classes of vehicles, the Truck Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-2p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-2P.svg">R2-2P</a>) plaque, Bus Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-2ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-2aP.svg">R2-2aP</a>) plaque, Truck-Bus Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-2bp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-2bP.svg">R2-2bP</a>) plaque, or Vehicles over X Tons Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-2cp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-2cP.svg">R2-2cP</a>) plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-3">Figure 2B-3</a>) shall be displayed below the Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) sign, except as provided in Paragraph 2 of this Section.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The legend of a Vehicle Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-2p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-2P.svg">R2-2P</a> series) plaque may be combined in a single sign and displayed below the SPEED LIMIT XX legend, similar to the Combined Maximum and Minimum Speed Limits (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-4a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-4a.svg">R2-4a</a>) sign (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b24">Section 2B.24</a>).</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> A different vehicle class legend may be substituted on the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-2p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-2P.svg">R2-2P</a> series plaque for other classes of vehicles not included in Paragraph 1 of this Section.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b23" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.23 Night Speed Limit Plaque (R2-3P)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.23 &middot; manual page 87 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b23">CA amended: 2B.23</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.23/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Where different speed limits are prescribed for day and night, both limits shall be posted.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> A Night Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-3p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-3P.svg">R2-3P</a>) plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-3">Figure 2B-3</a>) should be reversed using a white retroreflective legend and border on a black background.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> A Night Speed Limit plaque may be combined with or installed below the standard Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) sign.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b24" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.24 Minimum Speed Limit Plaque (R2-4P) and Combined Maximum and Minimum Speed Limits Sign (R2-4a)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.24 &middot; manual page 87 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b24">CA amended: 2B.24</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.24/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> A Minimum Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-4p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-4P.svg">R2-4P</a>) plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-3">Figure 2B-3</a>) shall be displayed only in combination with a Speed Limit sign. Where used, the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-4p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-4P.svg">R2-4P</a> plaque shall be mounted below a Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) sign.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Where engineering judgment determines that slow speeds on a highway might impede the normal and reasonable movement of traffic, the Minimum Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-4p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-4P.svg">R2-4P</a>) plaque may be installed below a Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) sign to indicate the minimum legal speed. In lieu of a sign assembly with the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a> sign and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-4p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-4P.svg">R2-4P</a> plaque, the Combined Maximum and Minimum Speed Limits (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-4a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-4a.svg">R2-4a</a>) sign may be used.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b25" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.25 Higher Fines Signs and Plaque (R2-6P, R2-10, and R2-11)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.25 &middot; manual page 87 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b25">CA amended: 2B.25</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.25/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
