EXCEPT ON SHOULDER (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r9-19p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R9-19P.svg">R9-19P</a>) plaque, and Q. EXCEPT LOCAL DELIVERY (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r5-2ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R5-2aP.svg">R5-2aP</a>) plaque.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> Appropriate combinations or groupings of these legends into a single sign, such as NO PEDESTRIANS BICYCLES MOTOR-DRIVEN CYCLES (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r5-10/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R5-10.svg">R5-10</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r5-10a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R5-10a.svg">R5-10a</a>) or NO PEDESTRIANS OR BICYCLES (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r5-10b/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R5-10b.svg">R5-10b</a>), may be used.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> If an exclusion is governed by vehicle weight, a Weight Limit sign (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b64">Section 2B.64</a>) should be used instead of a Selective Exclusion sign.</p>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> If used on a ramp to a freeway or expressway where pedestrian and bicyclist travel are prohibited by law or regulation, the NO PEDESTRIANS OR BICYCLES (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r5-10b/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R5-10b.svg">R5-10b</a>) sign should be installed in a location where it is clearly visible to any pedestrian or bicyclist attempting to enter the limited access facility from a street intersecting the ramp. In locations where a freeway or expressway is accessed from a ramp from a roadway parallel to the freeway or expressway, the sign should be placed in a location that clearly indicates the prohibition applies only to the freeway or expressway or to the ramp.</p>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> The Selective Exclusion sign should be placed on the right-hand side of the roadway at an appropriate distance from the intersection so as to be clearly visible to all road users turning into the roadway that has the exclusion. The NO PEDESTRIANS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r5-10c/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R5-10c.svg">R5-10c</a>) or No Pedestrian Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r9-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R9-3.svg">R9-3</a>) sign (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b57">Section 2B.57</a>) should be installed so as to be clearly visible to pedestrians who are at a location where an alternative route is available.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> The NO PEDESTRIANS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r5-10c/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R5-10c.svg">R5-10c</a>) or No Pedestrian Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r9-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R9-3.svg">R9-3</a>) sign may also be used at underpasses or elsewhere where pedestrian facilities are not provided.</p>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> The NO THRU TRAFFIC (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r5-12/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R5-12.svg">R5-12</a>) or NO THRU TRUCKS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r5-2b/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R5-2b.svg">R5-2b</a>) signs may be used at locations to prohibit through traffic from using a particular roadway or facility.</p>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> The EXCEPT LOCAL DELIVERY (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r5-2ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R5-2aP.svg">R5-2aP</a>) plaque may be mounted below the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r5-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R5-2.svg">R5-2</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r5-2b/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R5-2b.svg">R5-2b</a> sign.</p>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> The EXCEPT ON SHOULDER (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r9-19p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R9-19P.svg">R9-19P</a>) plaque may be used where such modes are allowed on a shoulder but not on the traveled way and placed at intersections with other roads and established paths or trails, where such vehicles or modes are expected to enter the highway.</p>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> The AUTHORIZED VEHICLES ONLY (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r5-11/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R5-11.svg">R5-11</a>) sign may be used at median openings and other locations to prohibit vehicles from using the median opening or facility unless they have special permission (such as law enforcement vehicles or emergency vehicles) or are performing official business (such as highway agency vehicles).</p>
</div>

<div class="subch">Do Not Enter, Wrong Way, One Way, And Related Signs And Plaques</div>

<div class="mfig-grid"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-12"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0107.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0107.webp" alt="Figure 2B-12. Selective Exclusion Signs and Plaques"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-12. Selective Exclusion Signs and Plaques <span class="src-page">p 107</span></figcaption></figure></div>

<span id="2b46" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.46 DO NOT ENTER Sign (R5-1)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.46 &middot; manual page 109 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b46">CA amended: 2B.46</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.46/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The DO NOT ENTER (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r5-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R5-1.svg">R5-1</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-13">Figure 2B-13</a>) shall be used at the following locations: A. Where a two-way roadway becomes a one-way roadway (see <a href="#fig-2b-18">Figure 2B-18</a>); B. The intersection of an interchange exit ramp with a crossroad as specified in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b48">Section 2B.48</a> (see <a href="#fig-2b-15">Figure 2B-15</a>); C. The intersection of a channelized or turning roadway with a two-way undivided crossroad; and D. Except as provided in Paragraph 4 of this Section, an intersection with a divided highway where the crossing functions as two separate intersections (see <a href="#fig-2b-14">Figure 2B-14</a>).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> A DO NOT ENTER sign should be installed at other locations where additional emphasis is needed where wrong-way movements are prominent or where the intersecting angle of roadways is such that the visibility of ONE WAY signs alone does not sufficiently convey the restriction.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> A DO NOT ENTER sign may be installed at an intersection with a divided highway where the crossing functions as a single intersection as shown in <a href="#fig-2b-20">Figure 2B-20</a>.</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> A DO NOT ENTER sign may be omitted on a low-speed urban street that is a divided highway at a crossing that functions as two separate intersections.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> An EXCEPT BICYCLES (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-7bp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-7bP.svg">R3-7bP</a>) plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-4">Figure 2B-4</a>) may be used with a DO NOT ENTER sign when counter-flow bicycle traffic is allowed.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> The DO NOT ENTER sign, if used, should be placed directly in view of a road user at the point where a road user could wrongly enter a divided highway, one-way roadway, or ramp. The sign should be mounted facing traffic that might enter the roadway or ramp in the wrong direction.</p>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> At a crossing with a divided highway that functions as a single intersection; the sign, if used, should be placed on the outside edge side of the roadway facing traffic that might enter the roadway in the wrong direction.</p>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> If the DO NOT ENTER sign would be visible to traffic to which it does not apply, the sign should be turned away from, or shielded from, the view of that traffic.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> A second DO NOT ENTER sign may be used, particularly where traffic approaches from an intersecting roadway (see <a href="#fig-2b-14">Figure 2B-14</a>).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b48">Section 2B.48</a> contains information regarding an optional lower mounting height for DO NOT ENTER signs that are located along an exit ramp facing a road user who is traveling in the wrong direction.</p>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2a/#2a12">Section 2A.12</a> contains the provisions for the use of continuously-operated or actuated LEDs to enhance the conspicuity of signs.</p>
</div>
<div class="mfig-grid"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-13"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0109.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0109.webp" alt="Figure 2B-13. DO NOT ENTER, WRONG WAY, ONE WAY, and Related Signs and Plaques"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-13. DO NOT ENTER, WRONG WAY, ONE WAY, and Related Signs and Plaques <span class="src-page">p 109</span></figcaption></figure></div>

<span id="2b47" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.47 WRONG WAY Sign (R5-1a)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.47 &middot; manual page 110 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b47">CA amended: 2B.47</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.47/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The WRONG WAY (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r5-1a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R5-1a.svg">R5-1a</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-13">Figure 2B-13</a>) may be used as a supplement to the DO NOT ENTER sign where a crossroad intersects a one-way roadway in a manner that does not physically discourage or prevent wrong-way entry (see <a href="#fig-2b-14">Figures 2B-14</a> and 2B-20).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> If used, the WRONG WAY sign should be placed at a location along the one-way roadway farther from the crossroad than the DO NOT ENTER sign (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b46">Section 2B.46</a>).</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The WRONG WAY sign should be placed on the same side of the road as the DO NOT ENTER sign.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b48">Section 2B.48</a> contains information regarding an optional lower mounting height for WRONG WAY signs that are located along an exit ramp facing a road user who is traveling in the wrong direction.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2a/#2a12">Section 2A.12</a> contains the provisions for the use of continuously-operated or actuated LEDs to enhance the conspicuity of signs.</p>
</div>
<div class="mfig-grid"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-14"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0110.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0110.webp" alt="Figure 2B-14. Locations of DO NOT ENTER and WRONG WAY Signing for Divided Highway Crossings that Function as Two Separate Intersections"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-14. Locations of DO NOT ENTER and WRONG WAY Signing for Divided Highway Crossings that Function as Two Separate Intersections <span class="src-page">p 110</span></figcaption></figure></div>

<span id="2b48" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.48 Wrong-Way Traffic Control at Interchange Ramps


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.48 &middot; manual page 111 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b48">CA amended: 2B.48</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.48/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> At interchange exit ramp terminals where the ramp intersects a crossroad in such a manner that wrong-way entry could inadvertently be made, the following signs shall be used (see <a href="#fig-2b-15">Figure 2B-15</a>): A. At least one ONE WAY sign for each direction of travel on the crossroad shall be placed where the exit ramp intersects the crossroad. B. At least one DO NOT ENTER sign shall be conspicuously placed near the downstream end of the exit ramp in positions appropriate for full view of a road user starting to enter wrongly from the crossroad. C. At least one WRONG WAY sign shall be placed on the exit ramp facing a road user traveling in the wrong direction.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> In addition, the following pavement markings should be used (see <a href="#fig-2b-15">Figure 2B-15</a>): A. On two-lane paved crossroads at interchanges, solid double yellow lines should be used as a center line for an adequate distance on both sides approaching the ramp intersections. B. Where crossroad channelization or ramp geometrics do not make wrong-way movements difficult, a lane- use arrow should be placed in each lane of an exit ramp near the crossroad terminal where it will be clearly visible to a potential wrong-way road user.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The following traffic control devices may be used to supplement the signs and pavement markings described in Paragraphs 1 and 2 of this Section: A. Additional ONE WAY signs may be placed, especially on two-lane rural crossroads, appropriately in advance of the ramp intersection to supplement the required ONE WAY sign(s). B. Additional WRONG WAY signs may be used. C. Slender, elongated wrong-way arrow pavement markings (see <a href="#fig-3b-21">Figure 3B-21</a>) intended primarily to warn wrong-way road users that they are traveling in the wrong direction may be placed upstream from the ramp terminus (see <a href="#fig-2b-15">Figure 2B-15</a>) to indicate the correct direction of traffic flow. Wrong-way arrow pavement markings may also be placed on the exit ramp at appropriate locations near the crossroad junction to indicate wrong-way movement. The wrong-way arrow markings may consist of pavement markings or bidirectional red-and-white raised pavement markers or other units that show red to wrong- way road users and white to other road users. D. Lane-use arrow pavement markings may be placed on the exit ramp and crossroad near their intersection to indicate the permissive direction of flow. E. Freeway entrance signs (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2d/#2d50">Section 2D.50</a>) may be used. F. Lane control signs or movement prohibition signs may be used on the approaches to the exit ramp. G. A Keep Right (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r4-7/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R4-7.svg">R4-7</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r4-7c/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R4-7c.svg">R4-7c</a>) may be used on a ramp median nose for wrong-way traffic control.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> On interchange entrance ramps where the ramp merges with the through roadway and the design of the interchange does not clearly make evident the direction of traffic on the separate roadways or ramps, a ONE WAY sign visible to traffic on the entrance ramp and through roadway should be placed on each side of the through roadway near the entrance ramp merging point as illustrated in <a href="#fig-2b-16">Figure 2B-16</a>.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> On interchange entrance ramps where the ramp merges with the through roadway and the design of the interchange does not clearly make evident the direction of traffic on the separate roadways or ramps a No Left Turn (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-2.svg">R3-2</a>) sign may be located on the left-hand side of the entrance ramp at the gore (see <a href="#fig-2b-16">Figure 2B-16</a>). If a No Left Turn (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-2.svg">R3-2</a>) sign is located on the left-hand side a supplemental <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-2.svg">R3-2</a> sign may be installed on the right-hand side of the entrance ramp.</p>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> On interchange entrance ramps where the ramp merges with the through roadway and the design clearly indicates the direction of flow, a ONE WAY sign may be placed visible to traffic on the entrance ramp and/or a NO TURNS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-3.svg">R3-3</a>) sign may be placed visible to traffic on the entrance ramp and through roadway at the gore area as illustrated in <a href="#fig-2b-16">Figure 2B-16</a>.</p>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> Where there are no parked cars, pedestrian activity, or other obstructions such as snow or vegetation, and if an engineering study indicates that a lower mounting height would address wrong-way movements on freeway or expressway exit ramps, a DO NOT ENTER sign(s) and/or a WRONG WAY sign(s) that is located along the exit ramp at a location downstream from the intersection with the crossroad facing a road user who is traveling in the wrong direction may be installed at a minimum mounting height of 3 feet, measured vertically from the bottom of the sign to the elevation of the near edge of the pavement. At the intersection with the crossroad, a WRONG WAY sign may be mounted at a minimum height of 3 feet on the same support on which a DO NOT ENTER sign is mounted at a height that complies with the provisions of <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2a/#2a15">Section 2A.15</a> (see <a href="#fig-2b-17">Figure 2B-17</a>).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b46">Sections 2B.46</a>, 2B.47, and 2B.49 contain further information on signing to avoid wrong-way movements at at-grade intersections on expressways.</p>
</div>
<div class="mfig-grid"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-15"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0112.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0112.webp" alt="Figure 2B-15. Example of Application of Regulatory Signing and Pavement Markings at an Exit Ramp Termination to Deter Wrong-Way Entry OR"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-15. Example of Application of Regulatory Signing and Pavement Markings at an Exit Ramp Termination to Deter Wrong-Way Entry OR <span class="src-page">p 112</span></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-16"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0113.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0113.webp" alt="Figure 2B-16. Example of Application of Regulatory Signing and Pavement Markings at an Entrance Ramp Terminal"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-16. Example of Application of Regulatory Signing and Pavement Markings at an Entrance Ramp Terminal <span class="src-page">p 113</span></figcaption></figure></div>

<span id="2b49" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.49 ONE WAY Signs (R6-1 and R6-2)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.49 &middot; manual page 114 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b49">CA amended: 2B.49</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.49/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Except as provided in Paragraph 6 of this Section, the ONE WAY (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R6-1__Sheet_1_of_2.svg">R6-1</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R6-2.svg">R6-2</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-13">Figure 2B-13</a>) shall be used to indicate streets or roadways upon which vehicular traffic is allowed to travel in one direction only.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> ONE WAY signs shall be placed parallel to the one-way street at all alleys and roadways that intersect one-way roadways as shown in <a href="#fig-2b-18">Figure 2B-18</a>.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> At the crossing of a roadway with a divided highway that functions as two separate intersections, ONE WAY signs shall be placed, visible to each crossroad approach, on the near right and far left corners of each intersection with the directional roadways (see <a href="#fig-2b-19">Figure 2B-19</a>).</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> At the crossing of a roadway with a divided highway that functions as a single intersection Keep Right (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r4-7/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R4-7.svg">R4-7</a>) signs (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b39">Section 2B.39</a>) and/or ONE WAY signs shall be installed (see <a href="#fig-2b-20">Figure 2B-20</a>). If Keep Right signs are installed, they shall be placed as close as practicable to the approach ends of the medians and shall be visible to traffic on the divided highway and angled (as needed) toward the applicable crossroad approach as shown in <a href="#fig-2b-20">Figure 2B-20</a>. If ONE WAY signs are installed, they shall be placed on the near right and far left corners of the intersection and shall be visible to each crossroad approach.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> At the crossing of a roadway with a divided highway, regardless of function as a single or separate intersections, ONE WAY signs may also be placed on the far right corner of the intersection as shown in <a href="#fig-2b-19">Figures 2B-19</a> and 2B-20.</p>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> ONE WAY signs may be omitted on the one-way roadways of divided highways, where the design of interchanges indicates the direction of traffic on the separate roadways.</p>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> An EXCEPT BICYCLES (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-7bp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-7bP.svg">R3-7bP</a>) plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-4">Figure 2B-4</a>) may be used with a ONE WAY sign when counter-flow bicycle traffic is allowed.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b48">Section 2B.48</a> contains information for the placement of ONE WAY signs at a crossroad with an interchange.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> If used at unsignalized intersections with one-way streets, ONE WAY signs shall be placed on the near right and the far left corners of the intersection facing traffic entering or crossing the one-way street (see <a href="#fig-2b-18">Figure 2B-18</a>).</p>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> If used at signalized intersections with one-way streets, ONE WAY signs shall be placed near the appropriate signal faces, on the poles holding the traffic signals, on the mast arm or span wire holding the signals, or at the locations specified for unsignalized intersections.</p>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> At unsignalized T-intersections where the roadway at the top of the T-intersection is a one-way roadway, ONE WAY signs shall be placed on the near-right and the far side of the intersection facing traffic on the stem approach (see <a href="#fig-2b-18">Figure 2B-18</a>).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> Where the central island of a roundabout allows for the installation of signs, ONE WAY signs may be used to direct traffic counter-clockwise around the central island (see <a href="#fig-2b-22">Figures 2B-22</a> and 2B-23).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">13</span> Where used on the central island of a roundabout, the mounting height of a ONE WAY sign should be at least 4 feet, measured vertically from the bottom of the sign to the elevation of the near edge of the traveled way.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">14</span> The BEGIN ONE WAY (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-6/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R6-6.svg">R6-6</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-13">Figure 2B-13</a>) may be used to notify road users of the beginning point of a one direction of travel restriction on the street or roadway. The END ONE WAY (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-7/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R6-7.svg">R6-7</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-13">Figure 2B-13</a>) may be used to notify road users of the ending point of a one direction of travel restriction on the street or roadway.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b50" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.50 Divided Highway Crossing Signs (R6-3 and R6-3a)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.50 &middot; manual page 116 &middot; <span class="badge rev">Revision 1 page</span> &middot; <a class="badge ca-same" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b50">CA MUTCD 2026: 2B.50</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.50/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> On unsignalized minor-street approaches from which both left turns and right turns are permitted onto a divided highway at a crossing that functions as two separate intersections (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2a/#2a23">Section 2A.23</a>), except as provided in Paragraph 2 of this Section, a Divided Highway Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R6-3.svg">R6-3</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-3a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R6-3a.svg">R6-3a</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-13">Figure 2B-13</a>) shall be used to advise road users that they are approaching an intersection with a divided highway (see <a href="#fig-2b-19">Figure 2B-19</a>).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> If the divided highway has a traffic volume of less than 400 AADT and a speed limit of 25 mph or less, at a crossing that functions as two separate intersections, the Divided Highway Crossing signs facing the unsignalized minor-street approaches may be omitted.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> A Divided Highway Crossing sign may be used on signalized minor-street approaches from which both left turns and right turns are permitted onto a divided highway to advise road users that they are approaching an intersection with a divided highway.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> If a Divided Highway Crossing sign is used at a four-leg intersection, the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R6-3.svg">R6-3</a> sign shall be used. If used at a T-intersection, the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-3a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R6-3a.svg">R6-3a</a> sign shall be used.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> The Divided Highway Crossing sign shall be located on the near right corner of the intersection, mounted beneath a STOP or YIELD sign or on a separate support.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> An additional Divided Highway Crossing sign may be installed on the left-hand side of the approach to supplement the Divided Highway Crossing sign on the near right corner of the intersection.</p>
</div>
<div class="mfig-grid"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-18"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0116.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0116.webp" alt="Figure 2B-18. Locations of ONE WAY Signs"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-18. Locations of ONE WAY Signs <span class="src-page">p 116</span></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-19"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0117.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0117.webp" alt="Figure 2B-19. ONE WAY Signing for Divided Highway Crossings that Function as Two Separate Intersections"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-19. ONE WAY Signing for Divided Highway Crossings that Function as Two Separate Intersections <span class="src-page">p 117</span></figcaption></figure></div>

<span id="2b51" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.51 Roundabout Circulation Plaque (R6-5P)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.51 &middot; manual page 118 &middot; <a class="badge ca-same" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b51">CA MUTCD 2026: 2B.51</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.51/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Where the central island of a roundabout or neighborhood traffic circle does not provide a reasonable place to install a sign as provided elsewhere in this Chapter, Roundabout Circulation (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-5p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R6-5P.svg">R6-5P</a>) plaques (see <a href="#fig-2b-13">Figure 2B-13</a>) should be placed below the YIELD signs on each approach.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Paragraph 6 of <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b39">Section 2B.39</a> contains information about the use of a Keep Right (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r4-7b/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R4-7b.svg">R4-7b</a>) sign in the central island of a neighborhood traffic circle.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> Paragraph 12 of <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2b/#2b49">Section 2B.49</a> contains information about the use of a ONE WAY (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R6-1__Sheet_1_of_2.svg">R6-1</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R6-2.svg">R6-2</a>) sign in the central island of a roundabout.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> At roundabouts where ONE WAY signs have been installed in the central island, Roundabout Circulation plaques may be placed below the YIELD signs on approaches to roundabouts to supplement the central island signs.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> The Roundabout Circulation plaque may be used at any type of circular intersection.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> Examples of regulatory and warning signs for roundabouts and neighborhood traffic circles are shown in <a href="#fig-2b-21">Figures 2B-21</a> through 2B-24.</p>
</div>

<div class="subch">Parking, Standing, Stopping, And Emergency Restriction Signs</div>

Example of Regulatory and Warning Signs for a Two-Lane Roundabout with Consecutive Double Lefts"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-23. Example of Regulatory and Warning Signs for a Two-Lane Roundabout with Consecutive Double Lefts <span class="src-page">p 121</span></figcaption></figure>

Example of Regulatory and Warning Signs for a Neighborhood Traffic Circle Rev. 1"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-24. Example of Regulatory and Warning Signs for a Neighborhood Traffic Circle Rev. 1 <span class="src-page">p 122</span></figcaption></figure></div>

<span id="2b52" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.52 Parking, Standing, and Stopping Signs (R7 and R8 Series)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.52 &middot; manual page 123 &middot; <span class="badge rev">Revision 1 page</span> &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b52">CA amended: 2B.52</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.52/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Parking signs pertain to the parking, standing, and stopping of vehicles along the roadway and in designated parking areas. They cover a wide variety of regulations, and only general guidance can be provided here. The word “standing” when used on the R7 and R8 series of signs refers to the practice of a driver keeping the vehicle in a stationary position while continuing to occupy the vehicle. The word “stopping” when used on the R7 and R8 series signs refers to any vehicle, occupied by a driver or not, that stops other than to avoid conflict with other traffic or to comply with official direction. Other types of activities such as active loading, active passenger loading, and/or waiting might be established in State or local codes for use on R7 and R8 series signs.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Parking signs are categorized as either (1) prohibiting parking or (2) permitting parking with restrictions on how parking is allowed.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The types of parking, standing, or stopping prohibitions that might be encountered include, but are not limited to: A. Prohibited at all times; B. Prohibited only at certain times of the day and/or days of the week; C. Prohibited with exceptions, such as for bus stops, loading/unloading zones, persons with disabilities, or electric vehicle charging stations; or D. Prohibited under certain conditions, such as Snow Emergency Routes.</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> Permissive parking signs allowing parking with restrictions include, but are not limited to: A. Parking only allowed for limited time duration (such as 30 minutes or for 1 hour); B. Metered parking requiring payment at an individual or a multi-space parking meter, or through electronic means such as by telephone or mobile application; C. Parking only for specific persons (such as those with disabilities or patrons or employees of a business) or specific vehicle types (such as electric vehicles, police/government vehicles, motorcycles, bicycles, or taxis); D. Angled or back-in angled parking when it is not commonly used in the area; E. Parking programs such as neighborhood/residential permits, school areas, or special events; and F. Emergency parking or stopping only.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b53" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.53 Design of Parking, Standing, and Stopping Signs


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.53 &middot; manual page 123 &middot; <span class="badge rev">Revision 1 page</span> &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b53">CA amended: 2B.53</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.53/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Parking, standing, or stopping signs (see <a href="#fig-2b-25">Figure 2B-25</a>) shall be rectangular or square.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Public agencies shall follow established law (State law, local ordinance, or regulation) as adopted by the authorized agency regarding what messages are allowed on parking signs.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The legend on parking signs shall state applicable regulations. Parking signs shall comply with the standards of shape, color, and location.</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> Prohibitive parking signs (see Drawing A in <a href="#fig-2b-25">Figure 2B-25</a> for some commonly used examples) shall be used where parking is prohibited at all times or at specific times. Except as otherwise provided in this Section, parking signs shall have a red legend and border on a white background and, when the parking prohibition symbol is used, the symbol “P” shall be black.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> Permissive parking signs (see Drawing B in <a href="#fig-2b-25">Figure 2B-25</a>) shall be used where only time-limited parking or parking in a particular manner is allowed. Permissive parking signs shall have a green legend and border on a white background.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> Parking information, should be displayed from top to bottom of the sign, as applicable, in the following order: A. The restriction or prohibition; B. The times of the day that it is applicable, if not all hours; C. The days of the week that it is applicable, if not every day; D. Qualifying or supplementary information; E. Exemptions to the restriction of prohibition; and F. Any tow-away message or symbol.</p>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> If the parking regulation applies to a limited area or zone, the limits of the regulation should be shown by arrows or supplemental plaques. If arrows are used and if the sign is at the end of a parking zone, there should be a single-headed arrow pointing in the direction that the regulation is in effect. If the sign is at an intermediate point in a zone, there should be a double-headed arrow pointing both ways. When a single sign is used at the transition point between two parking zones, it should display a right arrow and a left arrow pointing in the direction that the respective regulations apply.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> The times and days for which the parking regulations are in effect shall be posted if they are not in effect at all times of day or all days of the week.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> As an alternate to the use of arrows to show designated restriction zones, the following word messages may be used: BEGIN, END, HERE TO CORNER, HERE TO ALLEY, and THIS SIDE OF SIGN.</p>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> The R8 series signs (see Drawing A in <a href="#fig-2b-25">Figure 2B-25</a>) may be used where sufficient notice of a parking prohibition is satisfied by the use of single signs and are not needed to designate the beginning and end of a zone in which parking is prohibited or restricted. In rural and certain other areas the legends NO PARKING ON PAVEMENT (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r8-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R8-1.svg">R8-1</a>) or NO STOPPING ON PAVEMENT (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r8-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R8-5.svg">R8-5</a>) are generally suitable and may be used where parking or stopping is allowed on an unpaved shoulder or border adjacent to the paved portion of the road. If a roadway has an adjacent paved shoulder on which parking or stopping is allowed, the legend NO PARKING EXCEPT ON SHOULDER (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r8-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R8-2.svg">R8-2</a>) or NO STOPPING EXCEPT ON SHOULDER (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r8-6/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R8-6.svg">R8-6</a>) may be used. The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r8-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R8-3.svg">R8-3</a> symbol sign or the word message NO PARKING may be used to prohibit any parking along a roadway. Word legend supplemental plaques may be mounted below the NO PARKING signs or the word legend may be incorporated within signs whose sizes are increased accordingly. The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r8-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R8-3.svg">R8-3</a> series signs may include word legends such as ON PAVEMENT (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r8-3c/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R8-3c.svg">R8-3c</a>), ON BRIDGE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r8-3d/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R8-3d.svg">R8-3d</a>), ON TRACKS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r8-3e/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R8-3e.svg">R8-3e</a>), and EXCEPT ON SHOULDERS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r8-3f/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R8-3f.svg">R8-3f</a>).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> Where special parking restrictions are imposed during heavy snowfall or a declared snow emergency, a Snow Emergency Route (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-203/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-203.svg">R7-203</a>) sign (see Drawing A in <a href="#fig-2b-25">Figure 2B-25</a>) should be installed. The legend should be modified to display the specific regulations. The upper section of the sign should display the designation as a snow emergency route in a white legend and border on a red background.</p>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> If a fee is charged for on-street parking and payments are made at a multi-space parking meter, instead of individual parking meters for each parking space, Metered Parking (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-21/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-21.svg">R7-21</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-22/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-22.svg">R7-22</a>) signs (see Drawing B in <a href="#fig-2b-25">Figure 2B-25</a>) should be used to define the area where the multi-space parking meter applies. The Multi-Space Parking Meter (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-20/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-20.svg">R7-20</a>) sign (see Drawing B in <a href="#fig-2b-25">Figure 2B-25</a>) should be used at the meter location to direct road users to the meter.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">13</span> Where payments can be made electronically, such as by telephone or mobile application, the Mobile Parking Payment (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-21ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-21aP.svg">R7-21aP</a>) plaque (see Drawing B in <a href="#fig-2b-25">Figure 2B-25</a>) may be installed below or as part of the legend of a Metered Parking sign.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">14</span> If the metered parking is subject to a maximum time limit, the appropriate time limit (number of hours or minutes) shall be displayed on the Metered Parking (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-21/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-21.svg">R7-21</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-22/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-22.svg">R7-22</a>) signs and, except as provided in Paragraph 15 of this Section, on the Multi-space Parking Meter (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-20/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-20.svg">R7-20</a>) signs.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">15</span> Where the maximum time limit varies by the time of the day or by the day of the week, the display of the time limits may be omitted from the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-20/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-20.svg">R7-20</a> sign and, instead, be displayed on the multi-space parking meter so that they are visible to pedestrians as they make payments.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">16</span> Where parking spaces are reserved for persons with disabilities, the Accessible Parking (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-8/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-8.svg">R7-8</a>) sign (see Drawing D in <a href="#fig-2b-25">Figure 2B-25</a>) shall be used to designate the space and shall display the official International Symbol of Accessibility.</p>
<p><span class="pn">17</span> Where parking spaces that are reserved for persons with disabilities are designed to accommodate wheelchair vans, a VAN ACCESSIBLE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-8ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-8aP.svg">R7-8aP</a>) plaque (see Drawing D in <a href="#fig-2b-25">Figure 2B-25</a>) shall be mounted below the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-8/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-8.svg">R7-8</a> sign.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">18</span> Where parking spaces are designated for parking of electric vehicles, an Electric Vehicle Parking (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-111/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-111.svg">R7-111</a> series, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-112/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-112.svg">R7-112</a> series, and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-113/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-113.svg">R7-113</a>) sign (see Drawing E of <a href="#fig-2b-25">Figure 2B-25</a>) should be installed adjacent to the designated spaces. Where there is no time limit, the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-111/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-111.svg">R7-111</a> series sign should be used. Where parking is subject to a time limit, the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-112/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-112.svg">R7-112</a> series sign should be used.</p>
<p><span class="pn">19</span> Where parking spaces are only designated for charging of electric vehicles, an <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-113/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-113.svg">R7-113</a> sign or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-114/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-114.svg">R7-114</a> series sign (see Drawing E in <a href="#fig-2b-25">Figure 2B-25</a>) should be installed adjacent to the designated spaces.</p>
<p><span class="pn">20</span> Where additional restrictions apply while a vehicle occupies the designated space, the R7-113P series plaques should be installed below the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-113/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-113.svg">R7-113</a> sign or the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-114/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-114.svg">R7-114</a> series signs.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">21</span> Where parking is prohibited during certain hours and time-limited parking or parking in a particular manner is allowed during certain other time periods, the red Parking Prohibition and green Permissive Parking signs may be designed as follows (see Drawing C in <a href="#fig-2b-25">Figure 2B-25</a>): A. Two 12 x 18-inch parking signs may be used with the red Parking Prohibition (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-1.svg">R7-1</a>) sign installed above or to the left of the green Permissive Parking (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-108/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-108.svg">R7-108</a>) sign; or B. A single sign (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-200/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-200__Sheet_1_of_2.svg">R7-200</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-200a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-200a__Sheet_1_of_2.svg">R7-200a</a>) may be used.</p>
<p><span class="pn">22</span> At the transition point between two parking zones, a single sign (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-200/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-200__Sheet_1_of_2.svg">R7-200</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-200a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-200a__Sheet_1_of_2.svg">R7-200a</a>) or two signs mounted side-by-side may be used.</p>
<p><span class="pn">23</span> The words NO PARKING may be used as an alternative to the No Parking symbol (see the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-2a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-2a.svg">R7-2a</a> sign in Drawing A in <a href="#fig-2b-25">Figure 2B-25</a>).</p>
<p><span class="pn">24</span> Alternate designs for the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-107/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-107.svg">R7-107</a> sign may be developed such as the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-107a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-107a.svg">R7-107a</a> sign (see Drawing A in <a href="#fig-2b-25">Figure 2B-25</a>). Alternate designs may include, on a single sign, a transit logo, an approved bus symbol, a parking prohibition, the words BUS STOP, and an arrow. The reverse side of the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-107/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-107.svg">R7-107</a> series signs may display bus routing information for pedestrians.</p>
<p><span class="pn">25</span> A Tow-Away Zone (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-201p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-201P.svg">R7-201P</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-201ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-201aP.svg">R7-201aP</a>) plaque (see Drawing A in <a href="#fig-2b-25">Figure 2B-25</a>) may be mounted below any parking prohibition sign. The word legend TOW-AWAY ZONE may be incorporated into the parking prohibition sign in lieu of using a separate plaque.</p>
<p><span class="pn">26</span> The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-201p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-201P.svg">R7-201P</a> plaque may have a black or red symbol and border on a white background.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">27</span> When a legend other than that on the standard parking signs is necessary, letter height, symbol size, and basic sign layout should be consistent with those shown on the standard parking signs as detailed in the “Standard Highway Signs” publication (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/1a/#1a05">Section 1A.05</a>).</p>
<p><span class="pn">28</span> In general, the letter height of the principal legend on parking signs sized for urbanized applications should be at least 2 inches.</p>
</div>
<div class="mfig-grid"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-25"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0124.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0124.webp" alt="Figure 2B-25. Parking, Standing, and Stopping Signs and Plaques (R7 and R8 Series)"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-25. Parking, Standing, and Stopping Signs and Plaques (R7 and R8 Series) <span class="src-page">p 124</span></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-25"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0125.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0125.webp" alt="Figure 2B-25. Parking, Standing, and Stopping Signs and Plaques (R7 and R8 Series)"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-25. Parking, Standing, and Stopping Signs and Plaques (R7 and R8 Series) <span class="src-page">p 125</span></figcaption></figure></div>

<span id="2b54" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.54 Placement of Parking, Standing, and Stopping Signs


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.54 &middot; manual page 127 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b54">CA amended: 2B.54</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.54/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The efficacy of parking, standing, and stopping signs, when used on conventional roads in urbanized or developed environments, depends on their visibility and consistent placement along a street or within a particular block. It is often impracticable for the entire legend to be legible from similar distances as for other types of signs. Therefore, it is important that their conventional form be recognizable from an adequate distance such that the road user can obtain the information upon closer inspection.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> When signs with arrows are used to indicate the extent of the restricted zones, the signs should be set at an angle of not less than 30 degrees or more than 45 degrees with the line of traffic flow in order to be visible to approaching traffic.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> When signs are placed at the head of perpendicular parking stalls, the signs should be parallel to the roadway facing the parking stall.</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> Spacing of signs should be based on legibility, conspicuity, and sign orientation.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> If the zone is long, signs should be used at intermediate points within the zone.</p>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> If the signs are mounted at an angle of 90 degrees to the curb line, two signs should be mounted back to back at the transition point between two parking zones, each with an appended THIS SIDE OF SIGN (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r7-202p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R7-202P.svg">R7-202P</a>) supplemental plaque (see Drawing A in <a href="#fig-2b-25">Figure 2B-25</a>).</p>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> If the signs are mounted at an angle of 90 degrees to the curb line, signs without any arrows or appended plaques should be used at intermediate points within a parking zone, facing in the direction of approaching traffic. Otherwise, the standards of placement should be the same as for signs using directional arrows.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> Blanket parking regulations that apply to an entire jurisdiction may, if legal, be posted in the vicinity of the jurisdictional boundary lines. Blanket parking regulations that apply to a posted zone or district may, if legal, be posted at the entry points to the zone or district.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b55" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.55 Emergency Restriction Signs (R8-4 and R8-7)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.55 &middot; manual page 127 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b55">CA amended: 2B.55</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.55/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Emergency Restriction signs (see <a href="#fig-2b-26">Figure 2B-26</a>) shall be rectangular and shall have a black legend and border on a white background.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The EMERGENCY PARKING ONLY (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r8-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R8-4.svg">R8-4</a>) sign or the EMERGENCY STOPPING ONLY (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r8-7/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R8-7.svg">R8-7</a>) sign may be used to discourage or prohibit shoulder parking, particularly where scenic or other attractions create a tendency for road users to desire to stop temporarily.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/8b/#8b07">Section 8B.07</a> contains information for the use of the DO NOT STOP ON TRACKS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r8-8/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R8-8.svg">R8-8</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-8b-1">Figure 8B-1</a>) to discourage or prohibit parking or stopping on railroad or light rail transit tracks.</p>
</div>

<div class="subch">Pedestrian Signs</div>

<div class="mfig-grid"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-26"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0127.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0127.webp" alt="Figure 2B-26. Emergency Restriction Signs R8-4 R8-7"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-26. Emergency Restriction Signs R8-4 R8-7 <span class="src-page">p 127</span></figcaption></figure></div>

<span id="2b56" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.56 WALK ON LEFT FACING TRAFFIC and No Hitchhiking Signs (R9-1, R9-4, and R9-4a)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.56 &middot; manual page 128 &middot; <a class="badge ca-same" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b56">CA MUTCD 2026: 2B.56</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.56/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The WALK ON LEFT FACING TRAFFIC (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r9-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R9-1.svg">R9-1</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-27">Figure 2B-27</a>) may be used on highways where no sidewalks are provided.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> If used, the WALK ON LEFT FACING TRAFFIC sign should be installed on the right-hand side of the road where pedestrians walk on the pavement or shoulder in the absence of pedestrian pathways or sidewalks.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The No Hitchhiking (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r9-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R9-4.svg">R9-4</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-27">Figure 2B-27</a>) may be used to prohibit standing in or adjacent to the roadway for the purpose of soliciting a ride. The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r9-4a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R9-4a.svg">R9-4a</a> word message sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-27">Figure 2B-27</a>) may be used as an alternate to the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r9-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R9-4.svg">R9-4</a> symbol sign.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b57" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.57 Pedestrian Crossing Signs (R9-2 and R9-3)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.57 &middot; manual page 128 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b57">CA amended: 2B.57</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.57/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Pedestrian Crossing signs (see <a href="#fig-2b-27">Figure 2B-27</a>) may be used to limit pedestrian crossing to specific locations.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> If used, Pedestrian Crossing signs shall be installed to face pedestrian approaches.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> Where crosswalks are clearly defined, the CROSS ONLY AT CROSSWALKS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r9-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R9-2.svg">R9-2</a>) sign may be used to prohibit pedestrians from crossing at locations away from crosswalks.</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> The No Pedestrian Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r9-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R9-3.svg">R9-3</a>) sign may be used to prohibit pedestrians from crossing a roadway at an undesirable location or in front of a school or other public building where a crossing is not designated.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> The NO PEDESTRIAN CROSSING (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r9-3a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R9-3a.svg">R9-3a</a>) word message sign may be used as an alternate to the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r9-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R9-3.svg">R9-3</a> symbol sign. The USE CROSSWALK (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r9-3bp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R9-3bP.svg">R9-3bP</a>) supplemental plaque, along with an arrow, may be installed below either sign to designate the direction of the crossing.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> Pedestrians with vision disabilities might need features other than traffic control devices to provide effective communication of the prohibition of pedestrian crossing.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r9-3bp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R9-3bP.svg">R9-3bP</a> plaque should not be installed in combination with educational plaques.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b58" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.58 Traffic Signal Pedestrian and Bicyclist Actuation Signs (R10-1 through R10-4 and R10-24 through R10-26)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.58 &middot; manual page 128 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b58">CA amended: 2B.58</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.58/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Where manual actuation of a traffic signal is required for pedestrians or bicyclists to call a signal phase to cross the roadway, traffic signal signs applicable to pedestrian actuation (see <a href="#fig-2b-27">Figure 2B-27</a>) or bicyclist actuation (see <a href="#fig-9b-1">Figure 9B-1</a>) shall be mounted immediately above or incorporated into the push button detector units (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/4i/#4i05">Section 4I.05</a>).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Traffic signal signs applicable to pedestrians include: A. CROSS ONLY ON GREEN (symbolic circular green) (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-1.svg">R10-1</a>), B. CROSS ONLY ON (symbolic walk indication) SIGNAL (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-2.svg">R10-2</a>), C. Push Button for Walk Signal (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-3.svg">R10-3</a> series), and D. Push Button for Green Signal (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-4.svg">R10-4</a> series).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The following signs may be used as an alternate for the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-3.svg">R10-3</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-4.svg">R10-4</a> signs: A. Push Button to Cross Street Wait for Walk Signal (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-3a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-3a.svg">R10-3a</a>); or B. Push Button to Cross Street Wait for Green Signal (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-4a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-4a.svg">R10-4a</a>).</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> The name of the street to be crossed may be substituted for the word STREET in the legends on the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-3a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-3a.svg">R10-3a</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-4a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-4a.svg">R10-4a</a> signs.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> The finger in the push button symbol on the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-3.svg">R10-3</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-3a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-3a.svg">R10-3a</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-4.svg">R10-4</a>, and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-4a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-4a.svg">R10-4a</a> signs should point in the same direction as the arrow on the sign.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> Where symbolic pedestrian signal indications are used, an educational sign (R10-3b) may be used instead of the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-3.svg">R10-3</a> sign to improve pedestrian understanding of pedestrian indications at signalized intersections. Where word-legend pedestrian signal indications are being retained for the remainder of their useful service life, the legends WALK/DONT WALK may be substituted for the symbols on the educational sign R10-3b, thus creating educational sign R10-3c. The R10-3d educational sign may be used to inform pedestrians that the pedestrian clearance time is sufficient only for the pedestrian to cross to the median at locations where pedestrians cross in two stages using a median refuge island. The R10-3e educational sign may be used where countdown pedestrian signals have been provided. In order to assist the pedestrian in understanding which push button to push, the R10-3f through R10-3i educational signs that provide the name of the street to be crossed may be used instead of the R10-3b through R10-3e educational signs.</p>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-24/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-24.svg">R10-24</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-26/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-26.svg">R10-26</a> sign (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/9b/#9b20">Section 9B.20</a>) may be used where a push button detector has been installed exclusively to actuate a green phase for bicyclists.</p>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-25/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-25.svg">R10-25</a> sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-27">Figure 2B-27</a>) may be used where a push button detector has been installed for pedestrians to activate In-Roadway Warning Lights (see Chapter 4U) or flashing beacons that have been added to the pedestrian warning signs.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/4i/#4i05">Section 4I.05</a> contains information regarding the application of the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-32p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-32P.svg">R10-32P</a> plaque.</p>
</div>

<div class="subch">Traffic Signal Signs And Plaques</div>

<div class="mfig-grid"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-27"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0129.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0129.webp" alt="Figure 2B-27. Pedestrian Signs and Plaques"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-27. Pedestrian Signs and Plaques <span class="src-page">p 129</span></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-27"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0130.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0130.webp" alt="Figure 2B-27. Pedestrian Signs and Plaques"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-27. Pedestrian Signs and Plaques <span class="src-page">p 130</span></figcaption></figure></div>

<span id="2b59" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.59 Traffic Signal Signs and Plaques (R10-5 through R10-30)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.59 &middot; manual page 131 &middot; <span class="badge rev">Revision 1 page</span> &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b59">CA amended: 2B.59</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.59/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> To supplement traffic signal control, traffic signal (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-5.svg">R10-5</a> through <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-30/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-30.svg">R10-30</a>) signs (see <a href="#fig-2b-28">Figure 2B-28</a>) may be used to regulate road users.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Traffic signal signs may be installed at certain locations to clarify signal control. Among the legends that may be used for this purpose are: A. LEFT (RIGHT) ON GREEN ARROW ONLY (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-5.svg">R10-5</a>), B. STOP HERE ON RED (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-6/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-6.svg">R10-6</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-6a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-6a.svg">R10-6a</a>) for observance of stop lines, C. DO NOT BLOCK INTERSECTION (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-7/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-7.svg">R10-7</a>) for avoidance of traffic obstructions, D. USE LANE(S) WITH GREEN ARROW (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-8/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-8.svg">R10-8</a>) for obedience to lane-use control signals (see Chapter 4T), E. LEFT (RIGHT) TURN SIGNAL (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-10/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-10__Sheet_1_of_2.svg">R10-10</a>), F. U TURN SIGNAL (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-10a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-10a.svg">R10-10a</a>) for exclusive control of a U-turn movement, G. U TURN YIELD TO RIGHT TURN (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-16/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-16.svg">R10-16</a>), H. LEFT (RIGHT) TURN YIELD ON GREEN (symbolic circular green) (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-12/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-12.svg">R10-12</a>), I. LEFT (RIGHT) TURN YIELD ON FLASHING YELLOW ARROW (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-12a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-12a.svg">R10-12a</a>), and J. LEFT (RIGHT) TURN YIELD ON FLASHING RED ARROW AFTER STOP (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-27/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-27.svg">R10-27</a>).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> If used, the LEFT ON GREEN ARROW ONLY sign, the LEFT TURN SIGNAL sign, the LEFT TURN YIELD ON GREEN (symbolic circular green) sign, the LEFT TURN YIELD ON FLASHING YELLOW ARROW sign, or the LEFT TURN YIELD ON FLASHING RED ARROW AFTER STOP sign should be located adjacent to the left-turn signal face.</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> If used, the RIGHT ON GREEN ARROW ONLY sign, the RIGHT TURN SIGNAL sign, the RIGHT TURN YIELD ON FLASHING YELLOW ARROW sign, or the RIGHT TURN YIELD ON FLASHING RED ARROW AFTER STOP sign should be located adjacent to the right-turn signal face.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> A U TURN YIELD TO RIGHT TURN (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-16/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-16.svg">R10-16</a>) sign should be installed near the left-turn signal face if U-turns are allowed on a protected left-turn movement on an approach from which a right-turn GREEN ARROW signal indication is simultaneously being displayed to drivers making a right turn from the conflicting approach to their left.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> If used, a U TURN SIGNAL (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-10a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-10a.svg">R10-10a</a>) sign may be installed adjacent to the signal face that exclusively controls a U-turn movement.</p>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> If needed for additional emphasis, an additional LEFT TURN YIELD ON GREEN (symbolic circular green) (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-12/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-12.svg">R10-12</a>) sign with an AT SIGNAL (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-31p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-31P.svg">R10-31P</a>) supplemental plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-28">Figure 2B-28</a>) may be installed in advance of the intersection.</p>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> In situations where traffic control signals are coordinated for progressive timing, the Traffic Signal Speed (I1-1) sign may be used (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2h/#2h04">Section 2H.04</a>).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> The CROSSWALK—STOP ON RED (symbolic circular red) (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-23/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-23.svg">R10-23</a>) and STOP ON RED—YIELD ON FLASHING RED AFTER STOP (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-23a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-23a.svg">R10-23a</a>) signs (see <a href="#fig-2b-28">Figure 2B-28</a>) shall only be used in conjunction with pedestrian hybrid beacons (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/4j/#4j02">Section 4J.02</a>).</p>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> The EMERGENCY SIGNAL (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-13/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-13.svg">R10-13</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-28">Figure 2B-28</a>) shall be used in conjunction with emergency-vehicle traffic control signals (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/4m/#4m02">Section 4M.02</a>).</p>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> The EMERGENCY SIGNAL—STOP ON FLASHING RED (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-14/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-14.svg">R10-14</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-14a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-14a.svg">R10-14a</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-28">Figure 2B-28</a>) shall be used in conjunction with emergency-vehicle hybrid beacons (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/4n/#4n02">Section 4N.02</a>).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> If needed for extra emphasis, a STOP HERE ON FLASHING RED (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-14b/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-14b.svg">R10-14b</a>) sign may be installed with an emergency-vehicle hybrid beacon.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">13</span> The Left Turn Yield to Bicycles (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-12b/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-12b.svg">R10-12b</a>) sign shall be limited to applications where the conflicting bicyclist movement would be unexpected in direction, location, or similar condition that would tend to violate the expectation of a turning motorist.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">14</span> The Left Turn Yield to Bicycles sign should be located adjacent to the left-turn signal face.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p>If needed for additional emphasis, an additional Left Turn Yield to Bicycles sign with an AT SIGNAL (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-31p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-31P.svg">R10-31P</a>) supplemental plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-28">Figure 2B-28</a>) may be installed in advance of the intersection for motor vehicles.</p>
<p><span class="pn">16</span> Where conditions might warrant additional emphasis to drivers turning at a signalized intersection where potential pedestrian conflicts might not be readily apparent, a Turning Vehicles Yield to (Stop for) Pedestrians (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-15/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-15.svg">R10-15</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-15a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-15a.svg">R10-15a</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-28">Figure 2B-28</a>) may be used.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">17</span> The Turning Vehicles Stop for Pedestrians (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-15a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-15a.svg">R10-15a</a>) sign shall only be used in jurisdictions where laws, ordinances or resolutions specifically require that a driver must stop for a pedestrian.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">18</span> The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-15/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-15.svg">R10-15</a> series signs, where used, should be placed as follows: A. On the near right corner of the signalized intersection for right-turning vehicles. B. On the far left corner of the signalized intersection for the left-turning vehicles onto a two-way street. C. On the near left corner of the signalized intersection for left-turning vehicles from a one-way street onto a one-way street.</p>
</div>
<div class="mfig-grid"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-28"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0132.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0132.webp" alt="Figure 2B-28. Traffic Signal Signs and Plaques"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-28. Traffic Signal Signs and Plaques <span class="src-page">p 132</span></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-28"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0133.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0133.webp" alt="Figure 2B-28. Traffic Signal Signs and Plaques"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-28. Traffic Signal Signs and Plaques <span class="src-page">p 133</span></figcaption></figure></div>

<span id="2b60" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.60 No Turn on Red Signs (R10-11 Series, R10-17a, and R10-30)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.60 &middot; manual page 134 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b60">CA amended: 2B.60</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.60/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
