<p><span class="pn">01</span> Where a right turn on a circular red signal indication (or a left turn on a circular red signal indication from a one-way street to a one-way street) is to be prohibited, a NO TURN ON RED (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-11/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-11.svg">R10-11</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-11b/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-11b.svg">R10-11b</a>) word message sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-28">Figure 2B-28</a>) shall be used. A NO TURN ON RED (symbolic circular red) (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-11a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-11a.svg">R10-11a</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-28">Figure 2B-28</a>) shall be used when the approach is controlled by both circular red and red arrow indications.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> If used, the No Turn on Red sign should be installed near the appropriate signal head.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> A No Turn on Red sign should be considered when an engineering study finds that one or more of the following conditions exists: A. Inadequate sight distance to vehicles approaching from the left (or right, if applicable); B. Geometrics or operational characteristics of the intersection that might result in unexpected conflicts; C. An exclusive pedestrian or bicycle phase; D. An unacceptable number of conflicting pedestrian movements with right-turn-on-red maneuvers, especially involving children, older pedestrians, or persons with disabilities; E. More than three right-turn-on-red crashes reported in a 12-month period for the particular approach; or F. The skew angle of the intersecting roadways creates difficulty for drivers to see traffic approaching from their left (or right, if applicable).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> If an <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-11/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-11.svg">R10-11</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-11a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-11a.svg">R10-11a</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-11b/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-11b.svg">R10-11b</a>, or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-17a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-17a.svg">R10-17a</a> sign with conventional road size as shown in <a href="#fig-2b-1">Table 2B-1</a> is used on an approach on the far side of the intersection and the distance between the stop line and the sign is greater than 120 feet, then a duplicate sign shall be located on the near side of the intersection to supplement the sign on the far side of the intersection.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> When a no-turn-on-red restriction applies during certain time periods only, the following alternatives may be used: A. Movement Prohibition (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-1.svg">R3-1</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-2.svg">R3-2</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-4.svg">R3-4</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-18/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-18.svg">R3-18</a>, and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-27/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-27.svg">R3-27</a>) signs or NO TURN ON RED signs displayed by using a blank-out sign for the time period or one or more portion(s) of a particular cycle of the traffic control signal during which the prohibition is applicable; or B. Static signs incorporating a supplemental legend or with a supplemental <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-20ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-20aP__Sheet_1_of_2.svg">R10-20aP</a> plaque (see Figure 2B- 28) showing the hours and days during which the prohibition is applicable.</p>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> White LEDs may be used in the border and activated during periods of turn prohibition to enhance the sign conspicuity.</p>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> On signalized approaches with more than one right-turn lane, a NO TURN ON RED EXCEPT FROM RIGHT LANE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-11c/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-11c.svg">R10-11c</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-28">Figure 2B-28</a>) may be post-mounted at the intersection or a NO TURN ON RED FROM THIS LANE (with down arrow) (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-11d/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-11d.svg">R10-11d</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-28">Figure 2B-28</a>) may be mounted over the approximate center of the lane from which turns on red are prohibited.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> Where turns on red are permitted and the signal indication is a steady RED ARROW, the RIGHT (LEFT) ON RED ARROW AFTER STOP (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-17a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-17a.svg">R10-17a</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-28">Figure 2B-28</a>) should be installed adjacent to the RED ARROW signal indication.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> A RIGHT TURN ON RED MUST YIELD TO U-TURN (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-30/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-30.svg">R10-30</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-28">Figure 2B-28</a>) may be installed to remind road users that they must yield to conflicting U-turn traffic on the street or highway onto which they are turning right on a red signal after stopping.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b61" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.61 Ramp Metering Signs (R10-28 and R10-29)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.61 &middot; manual page 135 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b61">CA amended: 2B.61</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.61/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> When ramp control signals (see Chapter 4P) are used to meter traffic on a freeway or expressway entrance ramp, regulatory signs with legends appropriate to the control may be installed adjacent to the ramp control signal faces.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> For entrance ramps with only one controlled lane, an XX VEHICLE(S) PER GREEN (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-28/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-28.svg">R10-28</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-29">Figure 2B-29</a>) may be used to inform road users of the number of vehicles that are permitted to proceed during each short display of the green signal indication. For entrance ramps with more than one controlled lane, an XX VEHICLE(S) PER GREEN EACH LANE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-29/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-29.svg">R10-29</a>) (see <a href="#fig-2b-29">Figure 2B-29</a>) sign may be used to inform road users of the number of vehicles that are permitted to proceed from each lane during each short display of the green signal indication.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> Chapter 2L contains provisions for the use of blank-out or changeable message signs when the metering is limited by time, day, or condition.</p>
</div>

<div class="subch">Road Closed And Weight Limit Signs</div>

<div class="mfig-grid"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-29"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0135.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0135.webp" alt="Figure 2B-29. Ramp Metering Signs"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-29. Ramp Metering Signs <span class="src-page">p 135</span></figcaption></figure></div>

<span id="2b62" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.62 KEEP OFF MEDIAN Sign (R11-1)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.62 &middot; manual page 136 &middot; <a class="badge ca-same" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b62">CA MUTCD 2026: 2B.62</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.62/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The KEEP OFF MEDIAN (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r11-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R11-1.svg">R11-1</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-30">Figure 2B-30</a>) may be used to prohibit driving into or parking on the median.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The KEEP OFF MEDIAN sign should be installed on the left-hand side of the roadway within the median at random intervals as needed wherever there is a tendency for encroachment.</p>
</div>
<div class="mfig-grid"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-30"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0136.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0136.webp" alt="Figure 2B-30. Road Closed and Weight Limit Signs"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-30. Road Closed and Weight Limit Signs <span class="src-page">p 136</span></figcaption></figure></div>

<span id="2b63" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.63 ROAD CLOSED Sign (R11-2) and LOCAL TRAFFIC ONLY Signs (R11-3 Series, R11-4)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.63 &middot; manual page 137 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b63">CA amended: 2B.63</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.63/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The ROAD CLOSED (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r11-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R11-2.svg">R11-2</a>) sign should be installed where roads have been closed to all traffic (except authorized vehicles).</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> ROAD CLOSED—LOCAL TRAFFIC ONLY (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r11-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R11-3.svg">R11-3</a>) or ROAD CLOSED TO THRU TRAFFIC (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r11-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R11-4.svg">R11-4</a>) signs should be used where through traffic is not permitted, or for a closure some distance beyond the sign, but where the highway is open for local traffic up to the point of closure.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The Road Closed (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r11-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R11-2.svg">R11-2</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r11-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R11-3.svg">R11-3</a> series, and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r11-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R11-4.svg">R11-4</a>) signs (see <a href="#fig-2b-30">Figure 2B-30</a>) shall be designed as horizontal rectangles. These signs shall be preceded by the applicable Advance Road Closed warning sign with the secondary legend AHEAD and, if applicable, an Advance Detour warning sign (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/6h/#6h04">Section 6H.04</a>).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> An intersecting street name or a well-known destination may be substituted for the XX MILES AHEAD legend in urban areas.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> The word message BRIDGE OUT may be substituted for the ROAD CLOSED legend where applicable.</p>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> Where conditions allow for bicycle travel on the road beyond the point of closure to motor vehicles, an EXCEPT BICYCLES (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-7bp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-7bP.svg">R3-7bP</a>) plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-4">Figure 2B-4</a>) may be used with the ROAD CLOSED sign.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b64" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.64 Weight Limit Signs (R12-1 through R12-7)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.64 &middot; manual page 137 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b64">CA amended: 2B.64</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.64/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Weight limit signs (see <a href="#fig-2b-30">Figure 2B-30</a>) shall be used to indicate a section of highway or structure that has a vehicle weight restriction.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The units shown on any weight limit sign should be consistent within a State or region with respect to pounds or tons.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> Where the restriction applies to axle weight rather than gross load, the legend AXLE WEIGHT LIMIT XX TONS or AXLE WEIGHT LIMIT XX LBS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-2.svg">R12-2</a>) may be used.</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> In areas where multiple regulations are applicable, such as limiting both axle weight and gross vehicle weight, a WEIGHT LIMIT XX TONS PER AXLE, XX TONS GROSS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-4.svg">R12-4</a>) sign combining the necessary messages on a single sign may be used.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> Posting of specific load limits may be accomplished by use of the Weight Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-5.svg">R12-5</a>) symbol sign. A sign containing the legend WEIGHT LIMIT on the top two lines, and showing up to three different truck symbols and their respective weight limits for which restrictions apply may be used, with the weight limits displayed to the right of each symbol as XX T. A bottom line of legend stating GROSS WT may be included if needed for enforcement purposes.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> A specialized hauling vehicle is a single unit truck with multiple closely-spaced axles. Examples include dump trucks, construction vehicles, solid waste trucks and other hauling trucks. Specialized hauling vehicles typically have 4 to 7 axles.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> The Weight Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-6/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-6.svg">R12-6</a>) sign may be used to indicate vehicle weight restrictions for specialized hauling vehicles.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> The symbols shown on the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-5.svg">R12-5</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-6/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-6.svg">R12-6</a> Weight Limit sign shall apply to all trucks of that configuration (single-unit, single-trailer or multi-trailer) regardless of the shape of the vehicle. Symbolic representations of other vehicle shapes or modifications of standard symbols shall not be used.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> The facility type (such as “BRIDGE”) may be added to the legend of the sign to clarify the specific applicability of the weight limit.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> If the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-5.svg">R12-5</a> sign depicts only one single-unit vehicle symbol, the weight limit associated with that single- unit vehicle symbol shall apply to all single-unit vehicles, regardless of number of axles.</p>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> The weight limit associated with the single-trailer vehicle symbol shall apply to all single-trailer vehicles, regardless of number of axles or vehicle shape.</p>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> The weight limit associated with the multi-trailer vehicle symbol shall apply to all multi-trailer vehicles with two or more trailers, regardless of number of axles or vehicle shape.</p>
<p><span class="pn">13</span> A weight limit sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-30">Figure 2B-30</a>) shall be located at the applicable section of highway or structure.</p>
<p><span class="pn">14</span> An additional weight limit sign, with an advisory distance or directional legend, shall be located in advance of the applicable section of highway or structure so that prohibited vehicles can detour or turn around prior to the limit zone.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">15</span> An emergency vehicle is designed to be used under emergency conditions to transport personnel and equipment to support the suppression of fires and mitigation of other hazardous situations. Emergency vehicles are typically operated by fire departments and are primarily equipped for firefighting, but are also used to respond to and mitigate other hazardous situations in an emergency. They can create higher load effects compared to non- emergency vehicles of similar weight.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">16</span> The Emergency Vehicle Weight Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-7/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-7.svg">R12-7</a>) sign carrying the legend EMERGENCY VEHICLE WEIGHT LIMIT SINGLE AXLE XX TONS, TANDEM XX TONS, and GROSS XX TONS may be used to indicate vehicle weight restrictions for emergency vehicles.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">17</span> When the emergency-vehicle weight limit is displayed in the same assembly as the primary weight limit sign, the Emergency Vehicle Weight Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-7ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-7aP.svg">R12-7aP</a>) plaque shall be mounted below.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b65" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.65 Weigh Station Sign (R13-1)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.65 &middot; manual page 138 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b65">CA amended: 2B.65</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.65/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> An <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r13-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R13-1.svg">R13-1</a> sign with the legend TRUCKS OVER XX TONS MUST ENTER WEIGH STATION NEXT RIGHT (see <a href="#fig-2b-31">Figure 2B-31</a>) should be used to direct appropriate traffic into an inspection station.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r13-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R13-1.svg">R13-1</a> sign should be supplemented by the D8 series of guide signs (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2d/#2d51">Section 2D.51</a>).</p>
</div>

<span id="2b66" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.66 TRUCK ROUTE Sign (R14-1)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.66 &middot; manual page 138 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b66">CA amended: 2B.66</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.66/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The TRUCK ROUTE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-1.svg">R14-1</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-31">Figure 2B-31</a>) should be used to mark a route that has been designated to allow truck traffic.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2d/#2d20">Section 2D.20</a> contains information regarding the use of the TRUCK (M4-4P) auxiliary plaque (see <a href="#fig-2d-5">Figure 2D-5</a>) on a designated numbered alternative route.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b67" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.67 Hazardous Material Signs (R14-2 and R14-3)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.67 &middot; manual page 138 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b67">CA amended: 2B.67</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.67/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The Hazardous Material Route (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-2.svg">R14-2</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-31">Figure 2B-31</a>) may be used to identify routes that have been designated by proper authority for vehicles transporting hazardous material.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> On routes where the transporting of hazardous material is prohibited, the Hazardous Material Prohibition (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-3.svg">R14-3</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-31">Figure 2B-31</a>) may be used.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> If used, the Hazardous Material Prohibition sign should be installed on a street or roadway at a point where vehicles transporting hazardous material have the opportunity to take an alternate route.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b68" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.68 National Network Signs (R14-4 and R14-5)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.68 &middot; manual page 139 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b68">CA amended: 2B.68</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.68/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The signing of the National Network routes for trucking is optional.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> When a National Network route is signed, the National Network (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-4.svg">R14-4</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-31">Figure 2B-31</a>) shall be used.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The National Network Prohibition (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-5.svg">R14-5</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-31">Figure 2B-31</a>) may be used to identify routes, portions of routes, and ramps where trucks are prohibited. The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-5.svg">R14-5</a> sign may also be used to mark the ends of designated routes.</p>
</div>

<div class="subch">Other Regulatory Signs And Plaques</div>

<div class="mfig-grid"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-31"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0139.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0139.webp" alt="Figure 2B-31. Truck Signs MUTCD 11th Edition Page 139"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-31. Truck Signs MUTCD 11th Edition Page 139 <span class="src-page">p 139</span></figcaption></figure></div>

<span id="2b69" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.69 Photo Enforced Signs and Plaques (R10-18, R10-18a, R10-19P, R10-19aP)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.69 &middot; manual page 140 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b69">CA amended: 2B.69</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.69/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> A Traffic Laws Photo Enforced (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-18/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-18.svg">R10-18</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-32">Figure 2B-32</a>) may be installed at a jurisdictional boundary to advise road users that some of the traffic regulations within that jurisdiction are being enforced by photographic equipment.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> A Traffic Signal Photo Enforced (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-18a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-18a.svg">R10-18a</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-32">Figure 2B-32</a>) may be installed in advance of or at a traffic signal to advise road users that compliance with the signal is enforced by photographic equipment. A Signal Ahead (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w3-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W3-3.svg">W3-3</a>) sign and a Traffic Signal Photo Enforced (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-18a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-18a.svg">R10-18a</a>) sign may be used on the same approach provided that they are on separate supports.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> A Photo Enforced (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-19p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-19P.svg">R10-19P</a>) plaque or a PHOTO ENFORCED (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-19ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-19aP.svg">R10-19aP</a>) word message plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-32">Figure 2B-32</a>) may be mounted below a regulatory sign to advise road users that the regulation is being enforced by photographic equipment.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> The Traffic Signal Photo Enforced (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-18a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-18a.svg">R10-18a</a>) sign shall not be installed on approaches to signalized locations where red-light cameras are not present on any of the approaches to the signalized location.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> A Traffic Signal Photo Enforced (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-18a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-18a.svg">R10-18a</a>) sign shall not be installed on the same support in combination with a Signal Ahead (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w3-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W3-3.svg">W3-3</a>) sign.</p>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> If used below a regulatory sign, the Photo Enforced (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-19p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-19P.svg">R10-19P</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-19ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-19aP.svg">R10-19aP</a>) plaque shall be a rectangle with a black legend and border on a white background.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b70" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.70 Move Vehicles from Travel Lanes Sign (R16-4)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.70 &middot; manual page 140 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b70">CA amended: 2B.70</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.70/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> A STATE LAW MINOR CRASHES MOVE VEHICLES FROM TRAVEL LANES (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-4.svg">R16-4</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-33">Figure 2B-33</a>) may be installed in accordance with the provisions of <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2a/#2a01">Section 2A.01</a> to require motorists to move their vehicle out of the travel lanes if they have been involved in a crash.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> If the specific requirements of a State law vary, the word legend of the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-4.svg">R16-4</a> sign may be modified to reflect the appropriate law.</p>
</div>
<div class="mfig-grid"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-32"></span><span id="fig-2b-33"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0140.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0140.webp" alt="Figure 2B-32. Photo Enforcement Signs and Plaques; Figure 2B-33. Other Regulatory Signs"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-32. Photo Enforcement Signs and Plaques; Figure 2B-33. Other Regulatory Signs <span class="src-page">p 140</span></figcaption></figure></div>

<span id="2b71" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.71 Move Over or Reduce Speed Sign (R16-3)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.71 &middot; manual page 141 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b71">CA amended: 2B.71</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.71/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> A STATE LAW MOVE OVER OR REDUCE SPEED FOR VEHICLES STOPPED ON SHOULDER (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-3.svg">R16-3</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-33">Figure 2B-33</a>) may be installed in accordance with the provisions of <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2a/#2a01">Section 2A.01</a> to require motorists to change lanes and/or reduce speed when passing stopped emergency vehicles on the shoulder.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> If the specific requirements of a State law vary, the word legend of the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-3.svg">R16-3</a> sign may be modified to reflect the appropriate law.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b72" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.72 No Hand-Held Phone Use by Driver Signs (R16-15 and R16-15a)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.72 &middot; manual page 141 &middot; <a class="badge ca-same" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b72">CA MUTCD 2026: 2B.72</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.72/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> A STATE LAW NO HAND-HELD PHONE USE BY DRIVER (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-15/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-15.svg">R16-15</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-15a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-15a.svg">R16-15a</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-33">Figure 2B-33</a>) may be installed in accordance with the provisions of <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2a/#2a01">Section 2A.01</a> to notify drivers that they are prohibited from using hand-held telephones while driving.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> If the specific requirements of a State law vary, the word legend of the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-15/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-15.svg">R16-15</a> series signs may be modified to reflect the appropriate law.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b73" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.73 Headlight Use Signs (R16-5 through R16-11)


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.73 &middot; manual page 141 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b73">CA amended: 2B.73</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.73/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Some States require road users to turn on their vehicle headlights under certain weather conditions, as a safety improvement measure on roadways experiencing high crash rates, or in special situations such as when driving through a tunnel.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> <a href="#fig-2b-34">Figure 2B-34</a> shows the various signs that can be used for informing motorists of these requirements.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> A LIGHTS ON WHEN USING WIPERS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-5.svg">R16-5</a>) sign or a LIGHTS ON WHEN RAINING (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-6/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-6.svg">R16-6</a>) sign may be installed in accordance with the provisions of <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2a/#2a01">Section 2A.01</a> to inform road users of State laws regarding headlight use. Although these signs are typically installed facing traffic entering the State just inside the State border, they also may be installed at other locations within the State.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> If a particular section of roadway has been designated as a safety improvement zone within which headlight use is required, a TURN ON HEADLIGHTS NEXT XX MILES (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-7/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-7.svg">R16-7</a>) sign or a BEGIN DAYTIME HEADLIGHT SECTION (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-10/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-10.svg">R16-10</a>) sign should be installed at the upstream end of the section, and an END DAYTIME HEADLIGHT SECTION (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-11/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-11.svg">R16-11</a>) sign should be installed at the downstream end of the section.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> A TURN ON HEADLIGHTS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-8/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-8.svg">R16-8</a>) sign may be installed to require road users to turn on their headlights in special situations such as when driving through a tunnel. A CHECK HEADLIGHTS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-9/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-9.svg">R16-9</a>) sign may be installed downstream from the special situation to inform drivers that using their headlights is no longer required.</p>
</div>
<div class="mfig-grid"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-34"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0141.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/mutcd/p0141.webp" alt="Figure 2B-34. Headlight Use Signs"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-34. Headlight Use Signs <span class="src-page">p 141</span></figcaption></figure></div>

<span id="2b74" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.74 Seat Belt Symbol


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.74 &middot; manual page 142 &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b74">CA amended: 2B.74</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.74/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The seat belt symbol should not be used alone. If used, the seat belt symbol should be incorporated into regulatory sign messages for mandatory seat belt use.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The seat belt symbol is illustrated in the “Standard Highway Signs” publication (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/1a/#1a05">Section 1A.05</a>).</p>
</div>

<div class="subch">Barricades And Gates</div>


<span id="2b75" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.75 Barricades


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.75 &middot; manual page 143 &middot; <span class="badge rev">Revision 1 page</span> &middot; <a class="badge ca-same" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b75">CA MUTCD 2026: 2B.75</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.75/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Barricades may be used to mark any of the following conditions: A. The end of a roadway, B. A ramp or lane that is closed for operational purposes, or C. The permanent or semi-permanent closure or termination of a roadway.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> When used to warn and alert road users of the terminus of a roadway, other than in temporary traffic control zones, barricades shall meet the design criteria of <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/6k/#6k07">Section 6K.07</a> for a Type 3 Barricade, except that the colors of the stripes shall be retroreflective white and retroreflective red.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> An end-of-roadway marker or markers may be used as described in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2c/#2c73">Section 2C.73</a>.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> Appropriate advance warning signs (see Chapter 2C) should be used.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b76" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.76 Gates


<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.76 &middot; manual page 143 &middot; <span class="badge rev">Revision 1 page</span> &middot; <a class="badge ca-amend" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b76">CA amended: 2B.76</a> &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/us/2B.76/">short link</a></div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Gates described in this Section used for weather or other emergency conditions are typically permanently installed to enable the gate to be immediately deployed as needed to prohibit the entry of traffic to the highway segment(s).</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> A gate typically features a gate arm that is moved from a vertical to a horizontal position or is rotated in a horizontal plane from parallel to traffic to perpendicular to traffic. Traffic is obstructed and required to stop when the gate arm is placed in a horizontal position perpendicular to traffic. Another type of gate consists of a segment of fence (usually on rollers) that swings open and closed, or that is retracted to open and then extended to close.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> Gates are sometimes used to enforce a required stop. Some examples of such uses are the following: A. Parking facility entrances and exits, B. Private community entrances and exits, C. Military base entrances and exits, D. Toll plaza lanes, E. Movable bridges (see Chapter 4Q), F. Automated Flagger Assistance Devices (see Chapter 6L), and G. Grade crossings (see Part 8).</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> Gates are sometimes used to periodically close a roadway or a ramp. Some examples of such uses are the following: A. Closing ramps to implement counter-flow operations for evacuations, B. Closing ramps that lead to reversible lanes, and C. Closing roadways for weather events such as snow, ice, or flooding, or for other emergencies.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> Except as provided in Paragraph 6 of this Section, gate arms, if used, shall be fully retroreflective on both sides, have vertical stripes alternately red and white at 16-inch intervals measured horizontally as shown in <a href="#fig-8d-1">Figure 8D-1</a>. The width (which becomes the height of the retroreflective sheeting when the gate is in the down position) of the retroreflective sheeting on the front of the gate arm shall be at least 4 inches.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> If used on a one-way roadway or ramp, the retroreflective sheeting may be omitted on the side of the gate (or rolling fence) facing away from approaching traffic.</p>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> Where gate arms are used to block off ramps into reversible lanes or to redirect approaching traffic, the red and white striping may be angled such that the stripes slope downward at an angle of 45 degrees toward the side of the gate arm on which traffic is to pass.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> The gate arm shall extend across the approaching lane or lanes of traffic to effectively block motor vehicle, bicycle, and/or pedestrian travel as appropriate.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> When a gate that is rotated in a horizontal plane is in the position where it is parallel to traffic (indicating that the roadway is open), the outer end of the gate arm should be rotated to the downstream direction (from the perspective of traffic in the lane adjacent to the gate support) to prevent spearing if the gate is struck by an errant vehicle.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> If red lights are attached to a traffic gate, the red lights shall be steadily illuminated or flashed only during the period when the gate is in the horizontal or closed position and when the gate is in the process of being opened or closed.</p>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> Except as provided in Paragraph 6 of this Section, rolling sections of fence, if used, shall include either a horizontal strip of retroreflective sheeting on both sides of the fence with vertical stripes alternately red and white at 16-inch intervals measured horizontally to simulate the appearance of a gate arm in the horizontal position, or one or more Type 4 object markers (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/mutcd/2c/#2c73">Section 2C.73</a>), or both. If a horizontal strip of retroreflective sheeting is used, the bottom of the sheeting shall be located 3.5 to 4.5 feet above the roadway surface.</p>
</div>
